Relationships between parameters

Established relationships

Proved or definitionally valid inequalities and equalities.

VC Dimension / Effective VC Radius

Name: VC Dimension / Effective VC Radius

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Effective VC Radius

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes; this is immediate from the definition of effective VC radius.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If the effective VC radius is $k$, then every $k$-subset of the effective range is shattered. In particular one such subset is shattered, so the VC dimension is at least $k$.

References:

[vapnik1971uniform]

Largest Shattered Set / Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Name: Largest Shattered Set / Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Largest Shattered Set

Parameter 2: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The order-shattered sets are shattered sets.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

On the addressing family, VC dimension is floor(log_2 n), whereas the bit-first coordinate order has largest order-shattered set size one, so OSH_min=1. Thus no reverse affine-linear bound can hold; sync/verify_addressing_order_shattering.py checks the downshifting calculation.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every order-shattered set is shattered, so the largest size of a shattered set is at least the corresponding order-shattering parameter.

References:

[bollobas1995defect]

Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Name: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Parameter 2: Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Strong shattering is a special case of order shattering.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A strongly shattered set contains a full subcube and hence satisfies the order-shattering condition. Thus the order-shattering parameter is at least the largest strongly shattered set.

References:

[bollobas1995defect]

Interpolation degree / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Name: Interpolation degree / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Interpolation degree

Parameter 2: Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Lemma

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $\mathcal H$ contains a $d$-cube, restrict an interpolating polynomial to that cube. Every real-valued function on the cube must then be representable; in particular its parity function requires degree $d$. Hence $\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{ST}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[moran2015shattered]

Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set / Effective VC Radius

Name: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set / Effective VC Radius

Parameter 1: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Parameter 2: Effective VC Radius

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Lemma

Witness: Order-Shattering/Effective-VC-Radius Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Work on the effective range. If the effective VC radius is $d$, every $d$-subset is shattered. For each coordinate order, the relevant initial $d$-set is therefore order-shattered by the downshifting characterization. Thus every order has an order-shattered set of size $d$, and minimizing over orders gives $\mathrm{OSH}_{\min}(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{VCR}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[bollobas1995defect]

Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set / Interpolation degree

Name: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set / Interpolation degree

Parameter 1: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Parameter 2: Interpolation degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Lemma

Witness: Interpolation/Effective-VC-Radius Counterexample

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For the even-parity class C_even on three coordinates, OSH_min=2 while interpolation degree is 1; sync/verify_interpolation_conjecture.py checks every coordinate order and the interpolation rank.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every coordinate order, the standard monomials of the vanishing ideal form a basis for functions on $\mathcal H$, and their supports are the order-shattered sets for that order. Their degrees are thus at most the largest order-shattered-set size. Minimizing over orders gives $\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm{OSH}_{\min}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[moran2015shattered]

Worst Mistakes / VC Dimension

Name: Worst Mistakes / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Worst Mistakes

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The worst-case online mistake bound dominates VC dimension.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $S$ be a shattered set of size $d$. Even when the learner knows the whole presentation order, an adversary may present the points of $S$ one at a time and assign at each point the label opposite to the learner's prediction. After all $d$ rounds, shattering supplies a concept realizing the resulting labeling, so this is a valid target-consistent run with $d$ mistakes. Hence $\mathrm M_{\mathrm{worst}}\geq\mathrm{VC}$.

References:

[ben1997online]

Effective Range / Yang dimension

Name: Effective Range / Yang dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Yang dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Dimension bound from Yang's canonical suboplex.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Coordinates outside the effective range are constant on the class and may be deleted. On the remaining $\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)$ coordinates, Yang's canonical suboplex is a subcomplex of the boundary of the corresponding cross-polytope; every relevant section has dimension at most $\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)-1$. Since Yang dimension is one plus the largest possible homology dimension, $\mathrm{Y}(\mathcal H)\le \mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[yang2018homological]

Degeneracy / Minimum Degree

Name: Degeneracy / Minimum Degree

Parameter 1: Degeneracy

Parameter 2: Minimum Degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite $1$-inclusion graph.

Witness: Repetition-Free Teaching Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Degeneracy is the maximum, over induced subgraphs, of their minimum degrees. Taking the original graph as one candidate gives at least its minimum degree.

Densest Subgraph (twice) / Degeneracy

Name: Densest Subgraph (twice) / Degeneracy

Parameter 1: Densest Subgraph (twice)

Parameter 2: Degeneracy

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite $1$-inclusion graph.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For the half-interval family with $n\ge3$, the $1$-inclusion graph is an $n$-vertex path. Its degeneracy is $1$, while its double density is $2(n-1)/n>1$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every induced subgraph, its average degree is at least its minimum degree. Maximize the former over subgraphs to obtain the densest-subgraph parameter and the latter to obtain degeneracy.

References:

[haussler1994predicting]

Preference-Based Teaching Dimension / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Preference-Based Teaching Dimension / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Preference-Based Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

(for finite classes)

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For a finite class, fix a total preference order. To make the learner select a target $h$, the teacher must supply a teaching set that eliminates every hypothesis preferred to $h$; reversing the order turns these into the teaching sets of a recursive teaching plan. Conversely, the elimination order of a recursive teaching plan, read in reverse, is a preference order under which the same samples teach their assigned concepts. Both constructions preserve the maximum sample size, so minimizing gives $\mathrm{PBTD}=\mathrm{RTD}$.

References:

[gao2017preference]

For C_rtdp, RTD is 1; deleting the first coordinate gives the full two-cube and raises projected RTD to 2.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The monotone/projection variant takes the maximum recursive teaching dimension over projections. The full-domain projection is the original class, giving the inequality.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive]

Yang dimension / VC Dimension

Name: Yang dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Yang dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Theorem

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Restricting a minimal resolution to a largest shattered coordinate set yields a resolution of the full cube on that set, whose projective dimension is its cardinality. Hence the homological/Yang dimension is at least the VC dimension.

References:

[yang2018homological]

Random Equivalence Queries Complexity / VC Dimension

Name: Random Equivalence Queries Complexity / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes in the random-counterexample equivalence-query model.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Angluin and Dohrn prove that the VC dimension lower-bounds the expected number of equivalence queries when each counterexample is sampled at random from the disagreement set (their Theorem 25).

References:

[angluin2017random]

Minimum Teaching Set Size / Effective VC Radius

Name: Minimum Teaching Set Size / Effective VC Radius

Parameter 1: Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 2: Effective VC Radius

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite classes, by the definition of effective VC radius.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $d=\mathrm{VCR}(\mathcal H)$ and let $T$ be a teaching set of size less than $d$; coordinates outside the effective range may be discarded. Extend $T$ to a $d$-subset $S$ of the effective range. Since $S$ is shattered, there is a concept other than the target that agrees with it on $T$ and differs on a point of $S\setminus T$, contradicting that $T$ teaches the target. Thus every teaching set has size at least $d$.

Proper Ordered Sample Compression / Order Sample Compression

Name: Proper Ordered Sample Compression / Order Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Parameter 2: Order Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes. The singleton family gives an unbounded gap: $\mathrm{pOSC}=n-1$ while $\mathrm{OSC}=1$.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A proper ordered sample-compression scheme is an ordered sample-compression scheme with an additional properness requirement on reconstruction. Minimizing over the restricted class of schemes cannot improve the optimum.

Proper Ordered Sample Compression / Proper Labeled Sample Compression

Name: Proper Ordered Sample Compression / Proper Labeled Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Parameter 2: Proper Labeled Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes; proper ordered compression is a restricted form of proper labeled compression.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every proper order sample-compression scheme is, after forgetting its order requirement, a proper labeled sample-compression scheme. Minimizing over the smaller class of ordered schemes cannot give a smaller value.

References:

[darnstadt2016order]

VC Dimension / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: VC Dimension / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge c\sqrt{P_2}$

Variant:

Details:

Equivalently, $\mathrm{RTD}^p(\mathcal H)=O(\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)^2)$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Hu--Wu--Li--Wang prove $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal C)\leq 39.3752\,\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal C)^2-3.6330\,\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal C)$ for every finite binary class $\mathcal C$. Every coordinate projection $\mathcal H_{|S}$ has $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H_{|S})\leq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$, since any set shattered after projection was already shattered in $\mathcal H$. Apply their bound to every projection and maximize over $S$.

References:

[hu2017quadratic]

VC Dimension / Log Shattering

Name: VC Dimension / Log Shattering

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Shattering

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge \frac{cP_2}{\log n}$

Variant:

Details:

Sauer--Shelah counting bound.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $d=\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$ on a domain of size $n$, every shattered set has size at most $d$. Thus $|\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)|\le\sum_{i=0}^{d}\binom ni\le(en/d)^d$. Taking logarithms gives $d=\Omega(\log\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)/\log n)$, with the small-$n$ cases absorbed in the universal constant.

References:

[vapnik1971uniform]

Self-Directed Queries Complexity / Littlestone Dimension

Name: Self-Directed Queries Complexity / Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge \frac{cP_2}{\log n}$

Variant:

Details:

For a finite class on a domain of size $n$, $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{sd}}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{L}(\mathcal H)/\log_2 n$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Theorem 7 of Ben-David--Kushilevitz--Mansour proves $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm M_{\mathrm{sd}}(\mathcal H)\log_2 n$ for a finite domain of size $n$. Their tree proof takes a minimum-rank self-directed strategy tree and bounds its number of leaves by the binomial sum determined by its rank; the information-theoretic proof encodes a target by the at-most-$\mathrm M_{\mathrm{sd}}$ mistaken queried points. Rearranging gives the displayed inequality.

References:

[ben1997online]

Maximum Degree / Degeneracy

Name: Maximum Degree / Degeneracy

Parameter 1: Maximum Degree

Parameter 2: Degeneracy

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite $1$-inclusion graph.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every induced subgraph has maximum degree at most the maximum degree of the original graph, and its minimum degree is at most that maximum. Maximize over induced subgraphs in the definition of degeneracy.

References:

[haussler1988predicting] [alon1992colorings]

Teaching Dimension / Maximum Teaching Set Size

Name: Teaching Dimension / Maximum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Maximum Teaching Set Size

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Definition

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Teaching dimension is defined as the maximum, over concepts in the class, of the size of a smallest teaching set. This is precisely the maximum teaching-set size.

Distinguishing Range / Membership Query Complexity

Name: Distinguishing Range / Membership Query Complexity

Parameter 1: Distinguishing Range

Parameter 2: Membership Query Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

It is sufficient to query all coordinates in the distinguishing range.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $S$ be a smallest coordinate set on which the concepts of $\mathcal{H}$ have pairwise distinct restrictions. Querying the target on every coordinate in $S$ determines its restriction to $S$, and hence identifies the target. Therefore $\mathrm{Q}_m(\mathcal{H})\le |S|=\mathrm{R}(\mathcal{H})$.

Positive Teaching Dimension / Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Positive Teaching Dimension / Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Positive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A positive teaching set uniquely identifies its target among all concepts, hence is in particular non-clashing with every other assigned teaching set. Minimizing over positive teacher mappings yields the inequality.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Positive Teaching Dimension / Teaching Dimension

Name: Positive Teaching Dimension / Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Positive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For every n>=1, the full cube has positive teaching dimension infinity because its empty concept has no positive teaching set, while its ordinary teaching dimension is n. Thus the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Positive teaching sets are a restricted class of ordinary teaching sets. Therefore the minimum teaching-set size for each target, and hence its worst case, cannot decrease under the positivity restriction.

References:

[balbach2008measuring] [shinohara1991teachability] [goldman1995complexity]

Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

On the $n$-dimensional full cube, positive no-clashing teaching dimension is $n$, whereas ordinary no-clashing teaching dimension is $\lceil n/2\rceil$. The gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A positive non-clashing teacher mapping is a non-clashing teacher mapping with an additional restriction on its samples. Minimizing over the restricted class cannot lower the optimum.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Positive-Teaching Triangle

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For C_{+triangle}, positive RTD is 2 whereas ordinary RTD (equivalently finite-class PBTD) is 1; sync/verify_positive_teaching_triangle.py exhausts preference orders.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The positive recursive model requires every teaching sample used at every recursive stage to be positive. It is therefore a restriction of the ordinary recursive model, so its optimum cannot be smaller.

References:

[fallat2023batch]

Positive Teaching Dimension / Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Positive Teaching Dimension / Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Positive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For every n>=1, the full cube has positive teaching dimension infinity because its empty concept has no positive teaching set, while its positive recursive teaching dimension is n. Thus the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A positive teacher that distinguishes every target in the full class can be used at the first recursive stage. Recursive removal can only reduce the set of competitors, so the positive recursive optimum is no larger.

References:

[fallat2023batch]

Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension / Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension / Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Positive-Teaching Triangle

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For C_{+triangle}={110,101,011}, positive RTD is 2 whereas positive NCTD is 1; sync/verify_positive_teaching_triangle.py exhausts the finite teaching-plan and teacher-map choices.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A positive recursive teaching plan is non-clashing: for two concepts, the one removed first has a positive teaching set that distinguishes it from the later concept, which was still present at that stage. Thus its positive recursive plan is an admissible positive non-clashing mapping.

References:

[fallat2023batch]

Effective Range / Distinguishing Range

Name: Effective Range / Distinguishing Range

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Distinguishing Range

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every coordinate needed to distinguish two concepts belongs to their effective range.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A coordinate outside the effective range has the same value in every concept, so deleting it cannot merge two restrictions. A minimum distinguishing coordinate set may therefore be chosen inside the effective range, giving $\mathrm{R}(\mathcal{H})\le\mathrm{E}(\mathcal{H})$.

For finite concept classes.

Littlestone Dimension / Stable Unlabeled Complexity

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Stable Unlabeled Complexity

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Stable Unlabeled Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A conservative optimal online learner yields stable compression from its mistake examples.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the half-interval class $\mathcal I_n$, $\mathrm L=\Theta(\log n)$, while retaining the largest positive sample point (or nothing for an all-negative sample) is a stable unlabeled compression scheme of size one. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Use a conservative online learner with mistake bound $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)$, such as the standard optimal algorithm, and cycle it through a realizable sample until a full pass is mistake-free. Retain only the examples on which it made a mistake. There are at most $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)$; their labels are recoverable from the learner's successive mistaken predictions, and removing any unretained example leaves the final predictor unchanged. This gives a stable unlabeled compression scheme of that size.

References:

[hanneke2021stable]

No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Densest Subgraph (twice)

Name: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Densest Subgraph (twice)

Parameter 1: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Apply the average-degree lower bound to every subfamily. No-clashing teaching dimension is monotone under taking a subfamily, and the maximum average degree over those subfamilies is the densest-subgraph parameter.

[KSZ2019]

Degeneracy / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

A strongly shattered set of size $d$ supplies a contained $d$-cube in the $1$-inclusion graph. That cube is an induced subgraph of minimum degree $d$, so the graph degeneracy is at least $d$.

Maximum Positive Degree / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Littlestone Dimension / 2-Block Littlestone Dimension

Name: Littlestone Dimension / 2-Block Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: 2-Block Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A 2-block shattered tree is an ordinary shattered Littlestone tree.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The 2-block condition only restricts the coordinate labels allowed in an ordinarily shattered Littlestone tree. Therefore every tree counted by $\mathrm{B}_2(\mathcal H)$ is counted by $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)$, and $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{B}_2(\mathcal H)$.

Full Proof:

Proposition \ref{prop:block-littlestone-sandwich} in the survey.

2-Block Littlestone Dimension / VC Dimension

Name: 2-Block Littlestone Dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: 2-Block Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A VC-shattered set gives a level-constant 2-block tree.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $S=\{x_1,\ldots,x_d\}$ is VC-shattered, label every node on level $i$ by $x_i$. This is a shattered tree of depth $d$ and satisfies the 2-block constraint (indeed, it is level-constant everywhere). Hence $\mathrm{B}_2(\mathcal H)\ge\operatorname{VC}(\mathcal H)$.

Full Proof:

Proposition \ref{prop:block-littlestone-sandwich} in the survey.

Littlestone Dimension / Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Random counterexamples are a restriction of the adversarial equivalence-query model.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

An equivalence-query strategy that succeeds against every permissible counterexample also succeeds when the counterexample is selected randomly. Thus $\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{req}}(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathcal H)=\mathrm{L}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[littlestone1988learning]

Distinguishing Range / Log Size

Name: Distinguishing Range / Log Size

Parameter 1: Distinguishing Range

Parameter 2: Log Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Lemma

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $n$ singleton concepts, the distinguishing range is $n-1$, whereas $\log|\mathcal H|=\log n$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

On a distinguishing coordinate set $S$, the $|\mathcal{H}|$ concepts have pairwise distinct binary restrictions. There are only $2^{|S|}$ such restrictions, so $|\mathcal{H}|\le2^{|S|}$. Minimizing over $S$ gives $\mathrm{R}(\mathcal{H})\ge\log|\mathcal{H}|$.

VC Dimension / Projected Double Density or Projected Average Degree

Name: VC Dimension / Projected Double Density or Projected Average Degree

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Projected Double Density or Projected Average Degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: 1/2

Variant:

Details:

Down Shifting and Monotonicity

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every projection, the Haussler--Littlestone--Warmuth density lemma gives $|E|/|V|\le\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$. Since this parameter is twice that density (the average degree), it is at most $2\,\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$. Thus $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)\ge\tfrac12\,\mathrm{dens}^p(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[haussler1988predicting]

Sign Rank / Dual Sign Rank

Name: Sign Rank / Dual Sign Rank

Parameter 1: Sign Rank

Parameter 2: Dual Sign Rank

Variant:

Details:

For every sign matrix.

Witness: Universal Class

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $\mathcal U_n$, $\mathrm{SR}(\mathcal U_n)=n$, whereas Proposition 1 gives $\mathrm{DSR}(\mathcal U_n)\leq 2\lfloor\log_2 n\rfloor+1$. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If a fixed set of $k$ columns is linearly independent in every realizing matrix, then every realizing matrix has rank at least $k$. Thus the maximum such fixed $k$ (dual sign rank) is at most sign rank.

Projected VC Radius / VC Dimension

Name: Projected VC Radius / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Projected VC Radius

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Taking the largest effective VC radius over projections is exactly VC dimension.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $S$ is VC-shattered, the projection to $S$ is the full cube on $S$, so its effective VC radius is $|S|$; hence $\mathrm{VCR}^p\ge\mathrm{VC}$. Conversely, effective VC radius at least $d$ in a projection supplies a shattered $d$-set in that projection, and thus in the original class. Hence $\mathrm{VCR}^p=\mathrm{VC}$.

Projected VC Radius / Effective VC Radius

Name: Projected VC Radius / Effective VC Radius

Parameter 1: Projected VC Radius

Parameter 2: Effective VC Radius

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Definition

Witness: Repetition-Free Teaching Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The identity projection is among the coordinate projections. The maximum effective VC radius over all projections is therefore at least the effective VC radius of $\mathcal{H}$ itself.

Projected Strongly Shattered Dimension / VC Dimension

Name: Projected Strongly Shattered Dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Projected Strongly Shattered Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A set is shattered exactly when it becomes strongly shattered in its coordinate projection.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A set $S$ is shattered by $\mathcal H$ exactly when the projection $\mathcal H_{|S}$ contains the full $S$-cube. In that projection, $S$ is strongly shattered. Conversely, a strongly shattered set in any projection is shattered by the original class on the same coordinates. Taking maxima yields equality.

References:

[bollobas1995defect]

Definition

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The full-domain projection is among the projections optimized over by the projected parameter, so it includes every strongly shattered set of the original class.

Maximum Largest Order Shattered Set / VC Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Details:

Sandwich Theorem

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every domain order, the order-shattered sets are shattered, giving the upper bound by VC dimension. The order-shattering sandwich theorem supplies an order for which a maximum shattered set is order-shattered, giving the reverse bound.

Projected Minimum Degree / VC Dimension

Name: Projected Minimum Degree / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Projected Minimum Degree

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Project on the largest shattered set. The reverse inequality is false in general.

Witness: Rubinstein Minimum-Degree Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For every d at least 10, Corollary 44 gives VC(V_d)=d and ordinary minimum degree d+1; projected minimum degree is at least the ordinary one by the full projection.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $S$ is shattered with $|S|=d$, the projection to $S$ is the full $d$-cube and has minimum degree $d$. Hence projected minimum degree is at least VC dimension. Equality cannot be asserted in general: there are classes with VC dimension $10$ and minimum degree $11$, and the full projection is one candidate in the projected maximum.

References:

[rubinstein2007shifting]

Maximum Teaching Set Size / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Maximum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 2: Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Variant:

Details:

A corner of a contained cube must be distinguished on every cube coordinate.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For a contained $d$-cube, choose any corner. For each of the $d$ cube coordinates there is a neighbouring concept differing only there, so every teaching set for that corner must include all $d$ coordinates. Thus the maximum teaching-set size is at least $d$.

Majority Complexity / Littlestone Dimension

Name: Majority Complexity / Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Majority Complexity

Parameter 2: Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The pointwise-majority (halving) learner is one particular equivalence-query strategy.

Witness: Products of the Majority-Query Separation Class

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the t-fold product C_maj^t, majority-query complexity is 3t whereas Littlestone dimension (and unrestricted equivalence-query complexity) is 2t. The gap is unbounded with t.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The halving/majority procedure is one particular equivalence-query learner. Its query count is therefore at least the optimal equivalence-query complexity, which equals Littlestone dimension.

References:

[littlestone1988learning] [maass1992lower]

Littlestone Dimension / Membership and Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Membership and Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Membership and Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Allowing membership queries in addition to arbitrary equivalence queries can only help the learner.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Equivalence-query complexity equals Littlestone dimension. Allowing membership queries in addition to arbitrary equivalence queries enlarges the learner's permitted query family, so it cannot require more queries.

References:

[littlestone1988learning]

Membership and Equivalence Queries Complexity / Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Membership and Equivalence Queries Complexity / Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Membership and Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Partial equivalence queries include both total equivalence and one-point membership queries.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

On $\mathrm{ADDRESSING}_n$, a partial hypothesis which labels every address point negative identifies the target after its unique counterexample, so $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{partial}}=1$. Maass--Turán prove $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{ARB\text{-}MEMB}}=\Theta(\log n)$ for the same family. Thus the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

An arbitrary equivalence query is a total partial equivalence query, and a membership query is a partial query defined at one point. Thus partial equivalence queries include every query available in the membership-and-equivalence model.

References:

[maass1992lower]

Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity / Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Name: Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity / Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes, $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{partial}}(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Axis-Parallel Boxes

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

Fix any $d\ge1$ and let $n$ grow. Then $\mathrm{SDC}(\mathrm{BOX}_n^d)=2$ while $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{partial}}(\mathrm{BOX}_n^d)=\Theta(d\log n)$, so the gap is unbounded and rules out every reverse affine-linear bound.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Corollary 8(1) of Doliwa--Fan--Simon--Zilles states the chain $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{partial}}(\mathcal H)$. Its middle inequality is the simulation established by Goldman--Sloan: the partial-equivalence-query model is no more powerful than self-directed learning, so its optimal worst-case query count cannot be smaller.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive] [goldman1994power] [maass1992lower]

Unlabeled sample compression / Labeled Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Unlabeled sample compression

Variant:

Details:

Unlabeled compression is the more restrictive model because retained labels are not stored.

An unlabeled compression scheme is a labeled compression scheme with the additional restriction that retained labels are not stored. Minimizing over the more restrictive unlabeled schemes cannot improve the optimum.

[chalopin2022unlabeled]

Log Size / Size

Name: Log Size / Size

Size / VC Dimension

Name: Size / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite, nonempty concept classes.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If a set of size $d$ is shattered, its $2^d$ labelings are realized by distinct members of the class. Thus $|\mathcal H|\ge 2^d\ge d$, and maximizing $d$ gives the inequality.

References:

[vapnik1971uniform]

Size / Path Dimension

Name: Size / Path Dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Path Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Definition

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A full path on a set of size $d$ contains $d+1$ distinct projected concepts. Each is the projection of a distinct member of $\mathcal H$, so $|\mathcal H|\ge d+1\ge d$.

Recursive Teaching Dimension / Interpolation degree

Name: Recursive Teaching Dimension / Interpolation degree

Parameter 1: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Interpolation degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A recursive teaching plan yields a triangular interpolation basis.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Order the concepts by a recursive teaching plan of width $r=\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)$. For the teaching set $S_i$ of its $i$th concept $h_i$ among the remaining concepts, the product of literals agreeing with $h_i$ on $S_i$ has degree at most $r$, is nonzero at $h_i$, and vanishes at every later $h_j$. These polynomials give an invertible triangular evaluation matrix, hence a degree-at-most-$r$ interpolation basis.

References:

[moran2015shattered]

Self-Directed Queries Complexity / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Self-Directed Queries Complexity / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes, $\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Ben-David--Eiron Fixed-VC Self-Directed Family

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For any fixed $d\ge3$, Ben-David--Eiron's Lemma 1 gives $\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal C_n^d)\ge n$ while $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal C_n^d)=d$. Hu--Wu--Li--Wang's explicit quadratic VC-to-RTD bound gives $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal C_n^d)\le39.3752d^2-3.6330d$, independently of $n$. Thus $\mathrm{SDC}/\mathrm{RTD}$ is unbounded along this family.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Lemma 7 of Doliwa--Fan--Simon--Zilles (attributed there to Kuhlmann) gives $\mathrm{TS}_{\min}(\mathcal C)\leq\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal C)$ for every concept class. Since $\mathrm{SDC}$ is monotone under subclasses, their Lemmas 3--4 yield $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)=\max_{\mathcal C\subseteq\mathcal H}\mathrm{TS}_{\min}(\mathcal C)\leq\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive] [ben1998self] [hu2017quadratic]

Best Mistakes / Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Name: Best Mistakes / Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Best Mistakes

Parameter 2: Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Self-directed learning permits adaptive choice of the next instance.

Witness: Blockwise-Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

On the blockwise-addressing family, $\mathrm M_{\mathrm{best}}=\Theta(d)$ while $\mathrm{SDC}\leq2$; the gap therefore grows without bound with $d$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A self-directed learner can emulate any learner and favorable presentation sequence used for $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{best}}$ by choosing the same next instance itself. It may additionally adapt that choice to earlier feedback. Thus $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{sd}}(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{best}}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[ben1997online]

Effective Range / VC Dimension

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every coordinate of a shattered set takes both labels among members of $\mathcal H$, so it belongs to the effective range. Hence the effective range contains every shattered set.

Littlestone Dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $S=\{x_1,\ldots,x_d\}$ is shattered, use $x_1,\ldots,x_d$ as the successive levels of a complete binary tree. Every root-to-leaf labeling is realized by shattering, so this is a shattered tree of depth $d$.

Recursive Teaching Dimension / Effective VC Radius

Name: Recursive Teaching Dimension / Effective VC Radius

Parameter 1: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Effective VC Radius

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite classes, through minimum teaching-set size.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The first layer in the recursive teaching process is $\mathcal H$ itself, so $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{TS}_{\min}(\mathcal H)$. Every minimum teaching set has size at least $\mathrm{VCR}(\mathcal H)$, because a smaller one extends to a shattered set of size $\mathrm{VCR}(\mathcal H)$; see the proof of the minimum-teaching-set relation. Combining the two inequalities gives the claim.

Effective Range / Hamming Radius

Name: Effective Range / Hamming Radius

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Hamming Radius

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Definition

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Outside the effective range all concepts have the same label, so choose the ball center with that common label there. On the effective range every concept differs from the center on at most $E(\mathcal H)$ coordinates, giving Hamming radius at most the effective range.

Size / Yang dimension

Name: Size / Yang dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Yang dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Generator-count bound for Yang's canonical ideal.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For the singleton family, Size is n and Yang dimension is n-1, so the inequality is strict for every n>=2.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Yang's canonical ideal $I_{\mathcal H}^\star$ has exactly one minimal monomial generator for each $h\in\mathcal H$. For a nonempty class, the Taylor resolution on these $|\mathcal H|$ generators has length at most $|\mathcal H|-1$, so its projective dimension (Yang dimension) is at most $|\mathcal H|-1$. The empty class has Yang dimension $0$ by convention. Thus $\mathrm Y(\mathcal H)\le |\mathcal H|$ in all cases.

References:

[yang2018homological]

Size / Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Size / Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm Q_{\mathrm{peq}}(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Repeatedly propose any remaining candidate as a proper equivalence-query hypothesis. A negative answer supplies a counterexample and in particular eliminates the proposed candidate; after at most $|\mathcal H|-1$ negative answers, the unique remaining concept is the target.

References:

[angluin1988queries]

Log Shattering / Majority Complexity

Name: Log Shattering / Majority Complexity

Parameter 1: Log Shattering

Parameter 2: Majority Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Pajor's sandwich theorem bounds log size by log shattering, and halving uses at most log size.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The sandwich theorem gives $|\mathcal H|\leq |\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)|$. Taking binary logarithms yields $\log|\mathcal H|\leq\log|\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)|$. The pointwise-majority halving algorithm uses at most $\log|\mathcal H|$ queries, so its complexity is at most log shattering.

References:

[bollobas1995defect] [maass1992lower]

Labeled Sample Compression / VC Dimension

Name: Labeled Sample Compression / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Labeled Sample Compression

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: 1/5

Variant:

Details:

Floyd--Warmuth lower bound for general sample compression.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A labeled compression scheme is a general sample compression scheme. Floyd and Warmuth prove that every general compression scheme for a class of VC dimension $d$ has size at least $\lceil d/5\rceil$. Hence $\mathrm{LSC}(\mathcal H)\ge (1/5)\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[FW1995]

Best Mistakes / Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Name: Best Mistakes / Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Best Mistakes

Parameter 2: Minimum Largest Order Shattered Set

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes; both quantities admit a fixed-order tree-rank formulation.

Witness: Best-Mistakes/Order-Shattering Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For C_bo, the best fixed-order transductive mistake bound is 2 while the minimum largest order-shattered-set size is 1; sync/verify_best_mistakes_order_shattering_gap.py exhausts all 24 coordinate orders.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For a fixed domain order, Ben-David, Kushilevitz, and Mansour identify the optimal mistake count with the rank of the corresponding order-labeled consistency tree. An order-shattered set of size d yields a complete binary subtree of depth d in that tree, so its rank is at least d. Minimize over orders to obtain best mistakes at least the minimum largest order-shattered-set size.

References:

[ben1997online]

Radon Number / co-VC Dimension

Name: Radon Number / co-VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Radon Number

Parameter 2: co-VC dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, (\mathrm{Rad}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{coVC}(\mathcal H)).

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the full cube (2^{[n]}), (\mathrm{coVC}=0) whereas (\mathrm{Rad}=\lfloor\log_2(2n+2)\rfloor), which is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let the traces (t\mathbin\triangle{x_i}) for (i\in[d]) be a projected d-dimensional hollow star, with the centre trace t unrealizable. Choose realizing hypotheses (h_i). For a nontrivial partition (I\sqcup J=[d]), the version space fixing every coordinate (x_j), (j\in J), to (t(x_j)) contains all (h_i) with (i\in I); hence it contains the convex hull of that part. Symmetrically, the hull of the J-part is contained in the version space fixing the I-coordinates to t. The two version spaces have empty intersection, since their joint constraints prescribe the unrealizable trace t. Thus the (h_i) are Radon independent. Maximizing over hollow stars proves the inequality.

Full Proof:

Database-owned direct conversion of a hollow star into a Radon-independent set.

References:

[chase2024dual] [bousquet2020proper]

Log Size / Majority Complexity

Name: Log Size / Majority Complexity

Parameter 1: Log Size

Parameter 2: Majority Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite nonempty concept classes, with logarithms base $2$.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

On an incorrect pointwise-majority equivalence query, at least the ceiling half of the current version space disagrees with the proposal and is eliminated, leaving at most its floor half. The halving algorithm therefore needs at most $\lfloor\log_2|\mathcal H|\rfloor$ informative queries.

References:

[maass1992lower]

Hamming Radius / Oriented Diameter

Name: Hamming Radius / Oriented Diameter

Parameter 1: Hamming Radius

Parameter 2: Oriented Diameter

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Variant:

Details:

For finite classes, this is the metric inequality $B(\mathcal H)\ge D^+(\mathcal H)/2$.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

An oriented-diameter witness of size $d$ gives two concepts whose traces differ on all $d$ witness coordinates. For any Hamming-ball center, at least one of those concepts is at distance at least $d/2$ from it. Hence $B(\mathcal H)\ge \tfrac12 D^+(\mathcal H)$.

Threshold Dimension / Littlestone Dimension

Name: Threshold Dimension / Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Threshold Dimension

Parameter 2: Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge c\log P_2$

Multiplicative Constant: 1/2

Variant:

Details:

Shelah--Hodges threshold/Littlestone comparison. More precisely, $\mathrm T(\mathcal H)\geq\lfloor\log_2\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\rfloor$, which implies the displayed positive-constant logarithmic relation.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Theorem 8 of the cited source gives $\mathrm T(\mathcal H)\geq\lfloor\log_2\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\rfloor$. For every positive integer $m$, $\lfloor\log_2m\rfloor\geq\tfrac12\log_2m$ (and both sides are zero for $m=1$), giving the recorded logarithmic bound.

References:

[alon2020closure]

VC Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Name: VC Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Variant:

Details:

Sauer--Shelah bound. The full cube gives an unbounded reverse-linear separation: $\mathrm{VC}=n$ while $\mathrm{LSR}=\Theta(n/\log n)$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $d=\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$ and let $n$ be the domain size. Sauer--Shelah gives $|\mathcal H|\le\sum_{i=0}^{d}\binom ni\le(n+1)^d$. Therefore $d\ge\log(|\mathcal H|)/\log(n+1)$, which is the asserted bound up to the harmless $-1$ normalization in Log Size Ratio.

References:

[vapnik1971uniform]

Interpolation degree / Log Size Ratio

Name: Interpolation degree / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: Interpolation degree

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Variant:

Details:

Theorem

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $d=\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal H)$, monomials supported on at most $d$ of the $n$ coordinates span every function on $\mathcal H$. Since the function space has dimension $|\mathcal H|$, we have $|\mathcal H|\le\sum_{i=0}^{d}\binom ni\le(n+1)^d$. Hence $d\ge\log(|\mathcal H|)/\log(n+1)$, up to the Log Size Ratio normalization.

Projected Membership Queries Complexity / Membership Query Complexity

Name: Projected Membership Queries Complexity / Membership Query Complexity

Parameter 1: Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Membership Query Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Coded Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For C_code, ordinary membership-query complexity is ceil(log_2(n+1)), while the primary-coordinate projection is singletons plus the empty concept and has membership-query complexity n.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Projected membership-query complexity maximizes the membership-query complexity over projections. The full-domain projection is the original class, so it supplies the ordinary membership-query complexity as a candidate.

Projected Membership Queries Complexity / Star number

Name: Projected Membership Queries Complexity / Star number

Parameter 1: Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Star number

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A star witness forces one membership query per spoke.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

On a projected $d$-star with centre $h_0$ and leaves $h_i$ differing from $h_0$ only at $x_i$, an identification algorithm must query every $x_i$: if it omits $x_i$, the answers are identical for $h_0$ and $h_i$. Hence the membership-query complexity of that projection is at least $d$. Maximizing over projections gives $\mathrm{Q}_m^p(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{S}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[hanneke2015minimax]

Effective Range / Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Name: Effective Range / Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Each coordinate projection can be learned by querying only its effective coordinates.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every coordinate projection $\mathcal{G}$ of $\mathcal{H}$, querying all coordinates in the effective range of $\mathcal{G}$ distinguishes every pair of its concepts. Thus $\mathrm{Q}_m(\mathcal{G})\le\mathrm{E}(\mathcal{G})\le\mathrm{E}(\mathcal{H})$. Maximizing over projections gives $\mathrm{Q}_m^p(\mathcal{H})\le\mathrm{E}(\mathcal{H})$.

Size / Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Name: Size / Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Membership queries can eliminate at least one remaining candidate concept at each nonterminal step.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For any finite projection $\mathcal{G}$ of $\mathcal{H}$, repeatedly choose a coordinate on which two remaining concepts disagree. The answer eliminates at least one candidate, so at most $|\mathcal{G}|-1\le |\mathcal{H}|-1$ queries suffice. Hence $\mathrm{Q}_m^p(\mathcal{H})\le |\mathcal{H}|$.

Log Size / Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Log Size / Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Log Size

Parameter 2: Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A proper random-counterexample strategy halves the expected version-space size.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$ singletons together with the empty concept, $\log|\mathcal H|=\log(n+1)$ whereas the proper randomized equivalence-query complexity is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

At each stage, query a hypothesis minimizing its $L_1$ distance to the pointwise average of the current version space. For every target, a uniformly random counterexample leaves at most half the current hypotheses in expectation. Induction and Jensen's inequality for the concave logarithm then give expected remaining query cost at most $\log|\mathcal H|$.

Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every deterministic proper equivalence-query algorithm is a randomized one with degenerate randomness. Allowing randomization cannot increase the optimum, so deterministic complexity is at least randomized complexity.

Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Random Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Random Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Random proper equivalence-query algorithms are restricted to hypotheses from the class, whereas random equivalence-query algorithms may use arbitrary hypotheses. The restriction cannot lower the optimal expected or worst-case query complexity.

Recursive Teaching Dimension / Monotonic Minimum Teaching Set Size

Name: Recursive Teaching Dimension / Monotonic Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Monotonic Minimum Teaching Set Size

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes; this identifies recursive teaching dimension with its max-over-subclasses formulation.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

At every nonempty subfamily, the recursive teaching process removes a concept with a smallest teaching set; maximizing this minimum over subfamilies is exactly monotonic minimum teaching-set size.

[doliwa2014recursive]

Maximum Teaching Set Size / Relative Hitting Size

Name: Maximum Teaching Set Size / Relative Hitting Size

Parameter 1: Maximum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 2: Relative Hitting Size

Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Membership and Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Membership and Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Membership and Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $\mathrm{ADDRESSING}_n$, proper equivalence-query complexity is $n$, whereas the membership-plus-proper-equivalence complexity is $\Theta(\log n)$: query the bit coordinates to recover the injective address code. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Any learner using only proper equivalence queries is a valid learner in the model that additionally permits membership queries. The larger set of permitted queries cannot increase optimal complexity.

Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes. Strict on the singleton class: $\mathrm Q_{\mathrm{peq}}=n-1$ while $\mathrm Q_{\mathrm{eq}}=1$.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A proper equivalence-query learner may query only hypotheses from the class, whereas unrestricted equivalence queries allow arbitrary hypotheses. The restriction cannot decrease the optimal worst-case query complexity.

Membership Query Complexity / Relative Hitting Size

Name: Membership Query Complexity / Relative Hitting Size

Parameter 1: Membership Query Complexity

Parameter 2: Relative Hitting Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The query transcript identifying a fixed target is a teaching set for that target.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $n$ singleton concepts, membership-query complexity is $n-1$, while relative hitting size is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a target $h$ and run any membership-query learner that identifies it. The coordinates queried on the path leading to $h$ must distinguish $h$ from every other concept; otherwise that concept gives the same transcript and the learner cannot identify the target. Thus they form a teaching set for $h$, of size at most $\mathrm{Q}_m(\mathcal{H})$. Maximizing over $h$ yields $\mathrm{RHT}(\mathcal{H})\le\mathrm{Q}_m(\mathcal{H})$.

Littlestone Dimension / Prefix-$k$ Littlestone Dimension

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Prefix-$k$ Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Prefix-$k$ Littlestone Dimension ($k>1$)

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A prefix-$k$ shattered tree is an ordinary shattered Littlestone tree. For every fixed $k>1$, half-intervals give an unbounded separation; extending the notation to $k=1$ gives ordinary Littlestone dimension.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For every fixed $k>1$, the half-interval class has $\mathrm L=\Theta(\log n)$ and $\mathrm{PL}_k=1$. Extending notation to $k=1$ instead gives $\mathrm{PL}_1=\mathrm L$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The prefix-$k$ condition only restricts the coordinate labels allowed in an ordinary shattered Littlestone tree. Therefore $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{PL}_k(\mathcal H)$ for every fixed $k>1$. Extending the notation to $k=1$ gives the vacuous constraint and the identity $\mathrm{PL}_1=\mathrm L$.

Full Proof:

Proposition \ref{prop:prefix-littlestone-order} in the survey.

Size / Teaching Dimension

Name: Size / Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness: Repetition-Free Teaching Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix $h\in\mathcal H$. For each other $h'\in\mathcal H$, choose one coordinate on which h and $h'$ differ. The union of these at most $|\mathcal H|-1$ coordinates is a teaching set for h. Maximize over h.

Littlestone Dimension / Worst Mistakes

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Worst Mistakes

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Worst Mistakes

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite classes, the unrestricted tree rank defining Littlestone dimension dominates the regular-tree rank defining worst mistakes.

Witness: Littlestone/Worst-Order Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For C_lw, Littlestone dimension is 3 while the worst transductive fixed-order mistake bound is 2.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The worst-sequence/offline characterization maximizes the rank only over class trees induced by a fixed ordered sequence of instances: every depth level has one common queried instance. Each such regular class tree is a valid Littlestone tree, while Littlestone dimension maximizes over all instance-labeled class trees. Maximizing over the larger family gives $\mathrm L\geq\mathrm M_{\mathrm{worst}}$.

References:

[ben1997online]

Extended Teaching Dimension / Teaching Dimension

Name: Extended Teaching Dimension / Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Extended Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Definition: the maximization defining $\mathrm{XTD}$ includes targets in the class, for which specifying sets are teaching sets.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every target $h\in\mathcal H$, a specifying set for $h$ among all labelings is in particular a teaching set for $h$ within $\mathcal H$. Since $\mathrm{XTD}$ maximizes over a superset of targets, it is at least $\mathrm{TD}$.

References:

[hegedHus1995generalized]

Membership Query Complexity / Extended Teaching Dimension

Name: Membership Query Complexity / Extended Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Membership Query Complexity

Parameter 2: Extended Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A membership-query decision tree supplies a specifying set for every labeling. For finite classes with more than two concepts, Hegedüs also proves $Q_m\leq \mathrm{XTD}\log_2|\mathcal{H}|$.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a target labeling. Along a membership-query decision-tree path identifying it, the queried coordinates and their answers distinguish that labeling from every other member of the class, so they form a specifying set. The worst path length is therefore at least $\mathrm{XTD}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[hegedHus1995generalized]

Unlabeled Sample Compression / VC Dimension

Name: Unlabeled Sample Compression / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Unlabeled sample compression

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The inequality can be strict: Warmuth's $C_5$ class has $\mathrm{USC}=3$ and $\mathrm{VC}=2$.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If a set of $d$ points is shattered, its $2^d$ full labelings must compress to distinct unlabeled subsets: a single subset has only one reconstruction. Fewer than $d$ retained points offer only $\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\binom di<2^d$ subsets, so every unlabeled scheme has size at least $d$.

References:

[palvolgyi2021unlabeled]

Preference-Based Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Preference-Based Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Preference-Based Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every preference-based teaching mapping is no-clashing.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

Warmuth's C5 class has preference-based teaching dimension 3 and no-clashing teaching dimension 2, established by the exact C5 teaching-map calculation recorded in the value proofs.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let T_h be a preference-based teaching set for h. If two distinct concepts h,h' clashed, then h' would be consistent with T_h and h with T_h'. The first consistency forces h to be preferred to h', while the second forces h' to be preferred to h, contradicting strictness of the preference order. Thus the same mapping is no-clashing, so the preference-based teaching dimension is at least the no-clashing teaching dimension.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Effective Range / Monotonic Yang Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Monotonic Yang Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Monotonic Yang Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Consequence of the max-over-subfamilies construction of monotonic Yang dimension.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every subfamily $\mathcal G\subseteq\mathcal H$, the Yang bound gives $\mathrm Y(\mathcal G)\le\mathrm E(\mathcal G)$. Effective range is monotonic under subfamilies, so $\mathrm E(\mathcal G)\le\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$. Taking the maximum over $\mathcal G$ yields $\mathrm Y^*(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$.

Derivation:

max_subfamilies from Effective Range / Yang dimension

References:

[yang2018homological]

size / monotonic_yang_dimension

Name: size / monotonic_yang_dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Monotonic Yang Dimension

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

[yang2018homological]

Size / Hitting Size

Name: Size / Hitting Size

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Hitting Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A hitting set can select one coordinate from each nonempty concept.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For each nonempty $h\in\mathcal H$, choose one element $x_h\in h$. The set of chosen elements meets every nonempty concept, so $\mathrm{HT}(\mathcal H)\le |\mathcal H|$.

Effective Range / co-VC Dimension

Name: Effective Range / co-VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: co-VC dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A hollow star can use only coordinates in the effective range.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If a projected class contains a hollow $d$-star on S, then for each $x\in S$ the star's realizable neighbours include labelings that differ at x. Thus every x in S lies in the effective range, so $d\le \mathrm E(\mathcal H)$. Maximizing d gives $\mathrm{coVC}(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[bousquet2020proper]

Effective Range / Log Size Ratio

Name: Effective Range / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Effective range controls both the number of varying coordinates and class size.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

All concepts agree outside the effective range, hence $|\mathcal H|\le 2^{\mathrm E(\mathcal H)}$. For a nonempty domain, $\log(n+1)\ge1$, so $\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)\le\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$.

Name: Size / Information Complexity

Parameter 1: Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A finite class has a proper empirical-risk learner whose output carries at most its log-cardinality in information.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a deterministic proper empirical-risk minimizer with a fixed tie-breaking order on $\mathcal H$. Its output takes at most $|\mathcal H|$ values, hence $I(S;\mathcal A(S))\le H(\mathcal A(S))\le\log_2|\mathcal H|\le|\mathcal H|$ (with value 0 for a singleton class). Taking the infimum in the definition of information complexity preserves this upper bound.

References:

[pradeep2022information]

Effective Range / Oriented Diameter

Name: Effective Range / Oriented Diameter

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Oriented Diameter

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every coordinate in an oriented pair is active.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If an oriented pair (\emptyset,S) is realized in a projection, its two realizing concepts differ on every coordinate of S. Thus S is contained in the effective range. Maximizing gives (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm D^+(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Threshold Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Threshold Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Threshold Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every coordinate of a nontrivial threshold trace is active.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $n$ singleton concepts, effective range is $n$ while threshold dimension is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A threshold trace on S contains concepts with different labels at every coordinate of S. Hence every coordinate of S belongs to the effective range, so (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm T(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $n$ singleton concepts, effective range is $n$ while monotone recursive teaching dimension is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every coordinate projection has at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)) active coordinates. In every nonempty subfamily of such a projection, the full active-coordinate sample teaches any chosen concept, so each recursive teaching stage has a teaching set of size at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)). Taking the maximum over projections proves the bound.

Effective Range / Order Sample Compression

Name: Effective Range / Order Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Order Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $n$ singleton concepts, effective range is $n$ while order sample compression size is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix an order of the class. Retain every example of the input sample whose coordinate is active, and decode to the least class member consistent with the retained sample. Inactive examples have the same label in every class member, so this remains consistent with the original sample. At most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)) examples are retained.

Effective Range / Proper Stable Sample Compression

Name: Effective Range / Proper Stable Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Proper Stable Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$ singletons together with the empty concept, effective range is $n$, while proper stable sample-compression size is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Retain all active-coordinate examples of the sample and reconstruct the least consistent member of the class. This is proper, discarding inactive examples preserves consistency, and retaining every remaining example makes stability immediate. Its size is at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Proper Unlabeled Sample Compression

Name: Effective Range / Proper Unlabeled Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Proper Unlabeled Sample Compression

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$ singletons together with the empty concept, effective range is $n$ while proper unlabeled sample compression size is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Retain all active-coordinate examples and reconstruct a fixed least class member consistent with them. The retained examples include their observed labels, so the reconstruction is proper and consistent; inactive examples are irrelevant. The retained size is at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Proper Labeled Sample Compression
