Proof or Proof Sketch:

Retain the labeled examples on every active coordinate and reconstruct a fixed least consistent member of the class. Inactive examples are common to all class members, so this is consistent with the original sample and proper. Its size is at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Proper Stable Labeled Sample Compression

Name: Effective Range / Proper Stable Labeled Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Proper Stable Labeled Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$ singletons together with the empty concept, effective range is $n$ while proper stable labeled sample compression size is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The active-coordinate retention scheme is proper and consistent. It retains every active example, so removing an unretained (necessarily inactive) example does not change the retained sample or reconstruction; hence it is stable. Its size is at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)).

Size / Sign Rank

Name: Size / Sign Rank

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Sign Rank

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The rank of a matrix is at most its number of rows.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The incidence sign matrix of (\mathcal H) has (|\mathcal H|) rows. Any real matrix realizing its sign pattern has rank at most its number of rows, so minimizing this rank gives (\mathrm{SR}(\mathcal H)\le |\mathcal H|).

Effective Range / Minimum Teaching Set Size

Name: Effective Range / Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Minimum Teaching Set Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Full active-coordinate samples identify every concept.

Witness: Repetition-Free Teaching Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Two distinct concepts differ at an active coordinate. Thus the labels of any concept on the full effective range identify it within the class, giving it a teaching set of size at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)). In particular the minimum teaching-set size is at most that value.

Size / Dual Sign Rank

Name: Size / Dual Sign Rank

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Dual Sign Rank

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A linearly independent column set has size at most the number of rows.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every realizing matrix for the incidence sign matrix has (|\mathcal H|) rows. A set of linearly independent columns in such a matrix has size at most its rank, hence at most (|\mathcal H|). This applies to the fixed column set in the definition of dual sign rank.

Effective Range / Maximum Positive Degree

Name: Effective Range / Maximum Positive Degree

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Maximum Positive Degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every edge of the 1-inclusion graph uses an active coordinate.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A smaller neighbor counted by the dominance of a concept differs from it on one coordinate. Such a coordinate is active. Therefore the positive degree of every vertex, and hence its maximum, is at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Assign each concept all of its positive active coordinates.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Assign h the positive examples on all active coordinates in h. For two distinct concepts, one has an active coordinate absent from the other; the positive sample of that concept is inconsistent with the other. Hence this assignment is non-clashing and has size at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)).

Effective Range / Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Repeatedly remove an inclusion-maximal concept. Its positive labels on all active coordinates distinguish it from every remaining concept: no remaining concept can contain it, and each differs on a positive coordinate. These samples have size at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)), yielding a positive recursive teaching sequence with that bound.

Effective Range / Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Name: Effective Range / Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix an order of the class, retain every active-coordinate example, and decode to the least class member consistent with it. The decoder is proper and remains consistent with the full sample because inactive coordinates have a common label. This proper order-compression scheme retains at most (\mathrm E(\mathcal H)) examples.

Littlestone Dimension / $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension

Name: Littlestone Dimension / $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension ($k>2$)

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every $k$-block shattered tree is an ordinary shattered Littlestone tree.

Witness: Tagged Singletons

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The $k$-block condition only restricts the coordinate labels allowed in an ordinarily shattered Littlestone tree. Thus every tree counted by $\mathrm B_k(\mathcal H)$ is counted by $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)$, and $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm B_k(\mathcal H)$ for every fixed $k>2$.

Full Proof:

Proposition \ref{prop:block-littlestone-sandwich} in the survey.

$k$-Block Littlestone Dimension / VC Dimension

Name: $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension ($k>2$)

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A VC-shattered set gives a level-constant $k$-block tree.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $S=\{x_1,\ldots,x_d\}$ is VC-shattered, label every node on level $i$ by $x_i$. This is a shattered tree of depth $d$ and satisfies every $k$-block constraint. Hence $\mathrm B_k(\mathcal H)\ge\operatorname{VC}(\mathcal H)$ for every fixed $k>2$.

Full Proof:

Proposition \ref{prop:block-littlestone-sandwich} in the survey.

Distinguishing Range / Labeled Sample Compression

Name: Distinguishing Range / Labeled Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Distinguishing Range

Parameter 2: Labeled Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite binary concept classes.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A distinguishing coordinate set of size (\mathrm R(\mathcal H)) gives an injective binary trace map on (\mathcal H), so (\lceil\log_2|\mathcal H|\rceil\le\mathrm R(\mathcal H)). The mistake-driven halving construction gives a labeled sample-compression scheme of size at most (\lceil\log_2|\mathcal H|\rceil). Combining the two bounds yields (\mathrm{LSC}(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm R(\mathcal H)).

References:

[FW1995]

VC Dimension / Dual VC Dimension (logarithmic)

Name: VC Dimension / Dual VC Dimension (logarithmic)

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Dual VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge c\log P_2$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

Assouad proved $\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)\leq2^{\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)+1}-1$. Thus, when the dual VC dimension is positive, $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)\geq\tfrac12\log_2\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For $d=\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)\geq1$, Assouad's bound gives $\log_2\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)\leq d+1\leq2d$. The zero case has dual VC dimension zero.

References:

[assouad1983densite]

Dual VC Dimension / VC Dimension (logarithmic)

Name: Dual VC Dimension / VC Dimension (logarithmic)

Parameter 1: Dual VC Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge c\log P_2$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

Applying Assouad's bound to the dual class gives $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)\leq2^{\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)+1}-1$. Thus, when VC dimension is positive, $\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)\geq\tfrac12\log_2\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Universal Class

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Apply Assouad's theorem to $\mathcal H^*$ and use $(\mathcal H^*)^*=\mathcal H$ after identifying duplicate points. For positive $d=\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)$, the resulting logarithm is at most $d+1\leq2d$.

References:

[assouad1983densite]

Spherical Dimension / VC Dimension (affine)

Name: Spherical Dimension / VC Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Spherical Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 1

For every finite binary class, $\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)-1$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A shattered $d$-set yields the boundary of the $d$-dimensional cross-polytope as an antipodal simplicial $(d-1)$-sphere in the realizable-distribution complex, as shown in Lemma 2 of the cited paper.

[chornomaz2025spherical]

Yang Dimension / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Name: Yang Dimension / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Yang dimension

Parameter 2: Spherical Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: -\tfrac12

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite binary class, with Yang dimension computed over $\mathbb F_2$, $\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)\leq2\,\mathrm{Y}_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathcal H)-1$; equivalently $\mathrm{Y}_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathcal H)\geq(\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)+1)/2$.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the singleton family, Yang dimension is n-1 while spherical dimension is 1 for n>=3. Thus no reverse affine-linear bound of Yang dimension by spherical dimension can hold.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The antipodal realizable-distribution complex is the intersection of the Yang complex with its label flip. Its coindex is at most its Stiefel--Whitney height, then at most its Stanley--Reisner regularity minus one; the Kalai--Meshulam regularity inequality bounds that regularity by twice the Yang dimension.

Full Proof:

Full proof in the local Yang-dimension draft: ~/latex/YangDim/drafts/spherical_dimension.tex, Theorem thm:sd-vs-hd (unpublished).

Effective Range / Characteristic-p Yang Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Characteristic-p Yang Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Characteristic-p Yang Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every prime $p$.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

After deleting coordinates constant on the class, every relevant Yang-complex section lies in the boundary of the cross-polytope on $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ coordinates. Its homology has dimension at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)-1$ over every field, giving $\mathrm Y_p(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[yang2018homological]

Dual Antipodal VC Dimension / Dual VC Dimension

Name: Dual Antipodal VC Dimension / Dual VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Dual Antipodal VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Dual VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every dually shattered set is dually antipodally shattered. The full cube makes the comparison strict: $\mathrm{VC}^{*a}=\lfloor\log_2 n\rfloor+1$ whereas $\mathrm{VC}^{*}=\lfloor\log_2 n\rfloor$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For every full-cube dimension $n\ge1$, $\lfloor\log_2 n\rfloor+1$ is exactly one larger than $\lfloor\log_2 n\rfloor$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For each labeling, ordinary dual shattering supplies a point inducing that labeling itself, which is one of the two patterns allowed by dual antipodal shattering.

References:

[chornomaz2025spherical]

Extremal-Extension VC Dimension / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Parameter 2: Spherical Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite binary class, $\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)\leq2\,\mathrm{VC}_{\mathrm{extr}}(\mathcal H)-1$; equivalently $\mathrm{VC}_{\mathrm{extr}}(\mathcal H)\geq(\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)+1)/2$.

Extremal-Extension VC Dimension / VC Dimension

Name: Extremal-Extension VC Dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Extremal-Extension VC Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every extension contains the original class, so its VC dimension is at least that of the original class.

Witness: Extremal-Extension Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For C_ex, VC=2 and exhaustive enumeration of all same-domain shattering-extremal extensions gives extremal-extension VC dimension 3; sync/verify_extremal_extension_gap.py verifies both.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $\mathcal H\subseteq\mathcal E$, every set shattered by $\mathcal H$ is shattered by $\mathcal E$. Taking the minimum over extremal extensions gives the result.

List Replicability / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Name: List Replicability / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: List Replicability Number

Parameter 2: Spherical Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

For finite classes with $\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)\geq1$, $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H,\epsilon)\geq\lceil(\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)+3)/2\rceil$ for every $0<\epsilon<1/2$, hence $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H)\geq(\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)+3)/2$. The two-complementary-hypotheses case is excluded.

Witness: Universal Class

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

This is Theorem 10 of the cited paper. Taking the limit in $\epsilon$ gives the stated affine lower bound for the list replicability number.

Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / Star Number

Name: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / Star Number

Parameter 1: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Parameter 2: Star number

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, the combinatorial eluder dimension dominates the star number. The half-interval family gives an unbounded separation: $\mathrm{Edim}=n-1$ while star number is 2.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A star witness around a base hypothesis is a sequential eluder witness: every leaf differs from the base at its own point and agrees with it at all earlier star points.

Full Proof:

Theorem 8 (the star-number lower bound) of Li--Kamath--Foster--Srebro.

References:

[li2022understanding]

Effective Range / Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every point in an eluder witness is active, and the points in a witness sequence are distinct.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

At each witnessing point the base and witness hypothesis disagree, so the point lies in the effective range. A repeated point cannot be independent after it has appeared, because agreement with the base on the earlier copy forces agreement at its later copy.

References:

[li2022understanding]

Size / Combinatorial Eluder Dimension (affine)

Name: Size / Combinatorial Eluder Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: -1

Multiplicative Constant: 1

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite binary class, $|\mathcal H|\geq\mathrm{Edim}(\mathcal H)+1$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$-dimensional full cube, size is $2^n$ whereas combinatorial eluder dimension is $n$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The base hypothesis cannot serve as a witness. Moreover a witness hypothesis used at step i agrees with the base on all earlier points but differs at x_i, so it cannot have been used at an earlier step. Thus there are d+1 distinct hypotheses.

References:

[li2022understanding] [hanneke2024star_eluder]

Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / VC Dimension

Name: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, combinatorial eluder dimension directly dominates VC dimension. The addressing family gives an unbounded separation: $\mathrm{Edim}=n-1$ while $\mathrm{VC}=\log_2 n$ under the standard $n=2^d$ convention.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If S={x_1,...,x_d} is shattered, choose as base a hypothesis with label 0 on S. At step i, shattering supplies a hypothesis that is 1 at x_i and 0 at every earlier x_j. These form a sequential eluder witness of length d.

References:

[li2022understanding] [vapnik1971uniform]

Characteristic-p Yang Dimension / Characteristic-zero Yang Dimension

Name: Characteristic-p Yang Dimension / Characteristic-zero Yang Dimension

Parameter 1: Characteristic-p Yang Dimension

Parameter 2: Characteristic-zero Yang Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every prime $p$, $\mathrm{Y}_p(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{Y}_0(\mathcal H)$. For each fixed finite class, equality holds for all but finitely many primes. The only possible exceptional primes divide torsion in an integral homology group of a Hochster/interval complex. Universal coefficients do not by themselves compare two distinct positive primes, so no relationship between them is recorded here.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every integral complex $K$ in the Hochster formula, the universal coefficient exact sequence says that $\widetilde H_i(K;\mathbb F_p)$ is built from $\widetilde H_i(K;\mathbb Z)\otimes\mathbb F_p$ and $\operatorname{Tor}(\widetilde H_{i-1}(K;\mathbb Z),\mathbb F_p)$. A nonzero rational group has a free integral summand and therefore stays nonzero over every $\mathbb F_p$, giving $\mathrm{Y}_p\geq\mathrm{Y}_0$. There are finitely many relevant finite complexes and each has torsion at only finitely many primes, so outside their union the mod-$p$ and rational homology—and hence the Yang dimensions—agree. This does not compare two exceptional primes.

Full Proof:

Coefficient-field discussion and the Hochster/interval-homology formulation: ~/latex/YangDim/yang_dimension_combinatorics.tex, Section The cube-envelope lattice and the Gasharov--Peeva--Welker formula (unpublished local draft).

References:

[hatcher2002algebraic] [yang2018homological]

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every prime $p$.

A shattered set of size d induces the boundary of a d-dimensional cross-polytope in the Yang complex. Its top reduced homology is nonzero over every field, so Hochster's formula gives $\mathrm{Y}_p(\mathcal H)\geq d$. Maximize over shattered sets.

Size / Membership Query Complexity

Name: Size / Membership Query Complexity

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Membership Query Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm Q_m(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness: Warmuth's $C_5$ class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Maintain the version space of candidates. While it has two distinct concepts, choose a coordinate on which two candidates disagree and make a membership query there. Whichever answer is returned eliminates at least one candidate. After at most $|\mathcal H|-1$ queries only the target remains.

Size / Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

At any nonempty stage $\mathcal G$ of the recursive teaching process, choose one concept h with a minimum teaching set. As for ordinary teaching dimension, h has a teaching set of size at most $|\mathcal G|-1\leq|\mathcal H|-1$. Taking the maximum over stages proves the bound.

Size / Preference-Based Teaching Dimension

Name: Size / Preference-Based Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Preference-Based Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm{PBTD}(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix any strict preference order. For a target h, choose one disagreement coordinate against each concept preferred to h. The resulting sample rules out every more-preferred consistent concept and has size at most $|\mathcal H|-1$. Thus that order has teaching dimension at most $|\mathcal H|-1$, and minimizing over orders preserves the bound.

References:

[gao2017preference]

Size / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Size / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For each target h, choose one disagreement coordinate against every other concept. This gives a teaching set of size at most $|\mathcal H|-1$ which is consistent with no other concept. Assigning these samples to all targets is no-clashing, since two distinct concepts cannot both be consistent with either assigned sample.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Effective Range / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Name: Effective Range / Spherical Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Spherical Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: -\tfrac32

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class with $\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)\geq1$, $\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)\leq2\,\mathrm E(\mathcal H)-3$; equivalently $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq(\mathrm{sd}(\mathcal H)+3)/2$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

First quotient coordinates having the same evaluation vector on $\mathcal H$; the resulting domain has $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ points and preserves spherical dimension. The antipodal realizable-distribution complex is a finite subcomplex of the realizable-distribution complex and therefore has dimension at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)-1$. The local Borsuk--Ulam theorem in the cited paper forbids an antipodality-preserving map from $S^d$ whenever $2(\mathrm E-1)\leq d$. Hence $d\leq2\mathrm E-3$ for every admitted $d\geq1$.

References:

[chornomaz2025spherical]

Effective Range / Dual VC Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Dual VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Dual VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 1

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, the stronger counting bound $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq2^{\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)}-2$ holds. In particular $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal H)-1$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If d concepts are shattered in the dual, the $2^d$ label patterns on them are realized by distinct coordinates. All patterns except the all-zero and all-one patterns are nonconstant on those concepts, hence their coordinates are effective. Thus $\mathrm E\geq2^d-2$. Since $2^d-2\geq d-1$ for every d, this gives the recorded affine bound.

References:

[assouad1983densite]

Effective Range / Maximum Degree

Name: Effective Range / Maximum Degree

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Maximum Degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, the maximum degree of its 1-inclusion graph is at most its effective range.

Witness: Repetition-Free Teaching Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

At a fixed concept, two distinct 1-inclusion neighbors cannot differ from it on the same coordinate. Every coordinate supporting an incident edge is effective. Hence the vertex degree, and then the maximum degree, is at most \mathrm E(\mathcal H).

References:

[haussler1994predicting]

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, its one-inclusion average degree is at most its effective range.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every vertex of the 1-inclusion graph has degree at most \mathrm E(\mathcal H), since at most one neighbor can use each effective coordinate. Averaging the vertex degrees gives \mathrm{dens}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H).

[haussler1994predicting]

Effective Range / Dual Antipodal VC Dimension (affine)

Name: Effective Range / Dual Antipodal VC Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Dual Antipodal VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 1

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, the stronger counting bound $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq2^{\mathrm{VC}^{*a}(\mathcal H)-1}-1$ holds. In particular $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{VC}^{*a}(\mathcal H)-1$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If d concepts are dually antipodally shattered, each of the $2^{d-1}$ complementary pairs of label patterns is realized, up to a global label flip, by a coordinate. The pair consisting of the all-zero and all-one patterns is the only pair whose realizing coordinate can be constant on the chosen concepts. Every other pair is nonconstant, so its coordinate is effective. Thus at least $2^{d-1}-1$ effective coordinates occur. Since $2^{d-1}-1\geq d-1$ for every d, the stated affine bound follows.

References:

[chornomaz2025spherical]

Teaching Dimension / Balbach Teaching Dimension

Name: Teaching Dimension / Balbach Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Balbach Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite concept class, $\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{BTD}(\mathcal H)$. The singleton-plus-empty-set family gives an unbounded gap: $\mathrm{TD}=n$ while $\mathrm{BTD}=2$.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

At every Balbach iteration, an ordinary minimum teaching set for a target is still valid: it leaves no other consistent hypothesis, hence no other hypothesis surviving the size filter. Thus every iterated Balbach value is at most its ordinary teaching dimension; maximize at stabilization.

References:

[zilles2011models] [balbach2008measuring]

Variant:

Details:

For every finite concept class, $\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{STD}(\mathcal H)$. The singleton-plus-empty-set family gives an unbounded gap: $\mathrm{TD}=n$ while $\mathrm{STD}=1$.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

An ordinary minimum teaching set is admissible at the first subset-teaching step because it is inconsistent with every competing concept. The subset-teaching iterations only permit further reductions, so their stabilized maximum cannot exceed ordinary teaching dimension.

List Replicability / Dual Antipodal VC Dimension (affine)

Name: List Replicability / Dual Antipodal VC Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: List Replicability Number

Parameter 2: Dual Antipodal VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: -\tfrac12

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class and every $0<\epsilon<1/2$, $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H,\epsilon)\geq1+\lfloor\mathrm{VC}^{*a}(\mathcal H)/2\rfloor$. Hence $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H)\geq\tfrac12\mathrm{VC}^{*a}(\mathcal H)+\tfrac12$.

Witness: Universal Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For even n, the universal class has list replicability n/2+1 and dual antipodal VC dimension n. Thus the displayed affine lower bound is strict on this infinite subfamily.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

This is the dual-antipodal-VC consequence stated in Theorem 10. For an integer $d$, $1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor\geq d/2+1/2$; taking the accuracy limit yields the displayed affine bound.

Littlestone Dimension / Threshold Dimension (logarithmic)

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Threshold Dimension (logarithmic)

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Threshold Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge c\log P_2$

Multiplicative Constant: 1/2

Variant:

Details:

Shelah--Hodges threshold/Littlestone comparison: $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\geq\lfloor\log_2\mathrm T(\mathcal H)\rfloor$. Together with the existing reverse logarithmic relation, the two quantities are logarithmically comparable in both directions.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Theorem 8 of the cited source gives $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\geq\lfloor\log_2\mathrm T(\mathcal H)\rfloor$. For every positive integer $m$, $\lfloor\log_2m\rfloor\geq\tfrac12\log_2m$ (and both sides are zero for $m=1$), giving the recorded logarithmic bound.

References:

[alon2020closure]

Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / Log Size

Name: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / Log Size

Parameter 1: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{Edim}(\mathcal H)\geq\log_2|\mathcal H|$.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Repeatedly choose a coordinate on which the current version space disagrees, retain a largest label fibre, and continue until the version space is a singleton. In the binary case each step retains at least half the current class, so this constructs an eluder sequence of length at least $\log_2|\mathcal H|$.

References:

[hanneke2024star_eluder]

Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / Littlestone Dimension

Name: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension / Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Combinatorial Eluder Dimension

Parameter 2: Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every binary concept class, combinatorial eluder dimension dominates Littlestone dimension. The half-interval family gives an unbounded separation: $\mathrm{Edim}=n-1$ while $\mathrm L=\Theta(\log n)$.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a hypothesis $h^*$ and follow the branch it realizes in any shattered Littlestone tree. At each node, shattering supplies a hypothesis realizing the branch that instead takes the opposite edge there. It agrees with $h^*$ at all earlier node labels and disagrees at the current node, so the nodes on the branch form an eluder witness of the same length.

References:

[hanneke2024star_eluder]

Hanneke--Yang's Theorem 13 proves their finite-pool identity $\mathrm{XTD}(\mathcal H,m)=\min\{\mathrm S(\mathcal H),m\}$. Taking a pool containing the finite effective range gives $\mathrm{XTD}(\mathcal H)=\mathrm S(\mathcal H)$.

Minimum Star Number / VC Dimension

Name: Minimum Star Number / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Minimum Star Number

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, the minimum star number is at least VC dimension.

Witness: Universal Class

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the universal class with $n$ concepts, the recorded lower bound on minimum star number is $n/2-1$, while VC dimension is $\lfloor\log_2n\rfloor$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If a set of size d is shattered, then for any centre labeling g its restriction to that set is realized by one hypothesis, and each one-coordinate flip is realized by another. Thus this set is a g-centred star of size d; minimize over g.

References:

[hanneke2024star_eluder]

For the $n$-dimensional full cube, extremal-extension VC dimension is $n$, whereas dual VC dimension is $\lfloor\log_2n\rfloor$.

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The cited theorem gives $\mathrm{VC}^{*}(\mathcal E)\leq2\,\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal E)+1$ for every finite extremal class $\mathcal E$. Apply it to an extremal extension of $\mathcal H$ and use monotonicity of dual VC dimension under adding hypotheses; then minimize the extension VC dimension.

Sign Rank / Dual VC Dimension

Name: Sign Rank / Dual VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Sign Rank

Parameter 2: Dual VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{SR}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H^*)$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Transpose the incidence sign matrix. Proposition 1 gives that its dual sign rank is at least its VC dimension; dual sign rank is at most sign rank. Since sign rank is invariant under transposition, this yields $\mathrm{SR}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H^*)$.

References:

[alon2016sign]

Effective Range / Sign Rank

Name: Effective Range / Sign Rank

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Sign Rank

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 1

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{SR}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H)+1$.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Discard the coordinates on which all concepts agree. The remaining incidence matrix has $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ columns and therefore admits a realizing matrix of rank at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$. Reintroduce all constant columns using one additional rank-one direction. This realizes the original sign matrix with rank at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)+1$.

Effective Range / Littlestone Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every binary concept class, $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm L(\mathcal H)$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Follow any root-to-leaf path in a complete shattered Littlestone tree of depth d. The node labels on this path are distinct: repeating a point would make the branch that assigns it opposite labels unrealizable. At each node, the two child branches are realized by concepts with opposite labels there, so the point lies in the effective range. Thus the effective range contains at least d points.

References:

[littlestone1988learning]

Littlestone Dimension / Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every binary concept class, $\mathrm L(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{RL}(\mathcal H).

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

On the singleton-plus-empty class, Littlestone dimension is 1 whereas randomized Littlestone dimension is 1-2^{-n}, which is strictly smaller for every n>=1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Induct on the finite Littlestone dimension d. At the root of any shattered tree, the two restrictions have dimensions a,b at most d, and they cannot both equal d (otherwise their complete trees join to give dimension d+1). Thus $a\leq d$ and $b\leq d-1$ after ordering. Induction bounds each subtree's randomized value by its Littlestone dimension, so the root tree has half its expected branch length at most $1/2+(a+b)/2\leq d$. Taking suprema proves the claim; the infinite case follows by truncation.

Full Proof:

The randomized-tree definition is Definition 1 of [filmus2023randomized]; the comparison is a direct induction.

References:

[filmus2023randomized] [littlestone1988learning]

Randomized Littlestone Dimension / Littlestone Dimension

Name: Randomized Littlestone Dimension / Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: 0.5

Variant:

Details:

For every binary concept class, $\mathrm{RL}(\mathcal H)\geq\frac12\mathrm L(\mathcal H).

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A complete shattered Littlestone tree of depth d has every branch of length d, hence average branch length d. It witnesses randomized Littlestone dimension at least d/2. Take the supremum over d.

Full Proof:

Immediate from Definition 1 of [filmus2023randomized] and the ordinary shattered-tree definition.

References:

[filmus2023randomized] [littlestone1988learning]

Log Size / Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Name: Log Size / Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 1: Log Size

Parameter 2: Randomized Littlestone Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: 2

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite binary concept class, $\log_2|\mathcal H|\geq2\,\mathrm{RL}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Distinct leaves of a shattered tree require distinct realizing hypotheses, so it has at most $|\mathcal H|$ leaves. A uniform random root-to-leaf walk reaches a leaf at depth d with probability $2^{-d}$; its expected depth is therefore the entropy of this leaf distribution, at most $\log_2|\mathcal H|$. Divide by two and take the supremum over shattered trees.

Full Proof:

Definition 1 of [filmus2023randomized], followed by the elementary leaf-entropy bound.

References:

[filmus2023randomized]

Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every globally identifying teaching map is no-clashing.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$ singletons together with the empty concept, teaching dimension is $n$ while no-clashing teaching dimension is 1.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For each $h\in\mathcal H$, choose a teaching set $T(h)$ of size at most $\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H)$. If $h\ne h'$, then $T(h)$ is inconsistent with $h'$ because it identifies $h$ uniquely in the whole class. Thus no pair can clash, and the same map witnesses $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H)$.

Full Proof:

Full proof in the LaTeX survey: Proposition prop:td-low-nctd.

References:

[goldman1995complexity] [KSZ2019]

Recursive Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Name: Recursive Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every recursive teaching map is no-clashing.

Witness: Quadratic-Residue Tournament Classes

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the quadratic-residue tournament classes $\mathcal Q_p$, $\mathrm{NCTD}=1$ while $\mathrm{RTD}\geq\lfloor\tfrac12\log_2p-\log_2\log_2p\rfloor$ for infinitely many primes $p\equiv3\pmod4$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Take a recursive teaching order and, for each concept h, a teaching set T(h) identifying h within the then-current remaining class. For two distinct concepts h,h', assume h is removed first. Then h' is still present when T(h) is chosen, so T(h) is inconsistent with h'. Thus no pair can clash. The same map has width at most $\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)$, proving $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)$.

Full Proof:

Full proof in the LaTeX survey: Proposition prop:rtd-low-nctd.

References:

[zilles2011models] [KSZ2019] [simon2023tournaments]

Proper Ordered Sample Compression / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Proper Ordered Sample Compression / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Parameter 2: Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes. This is the fixed-proper-reconstruction form of the restriction and teaching-plan proof underlying Theorem 4 of Darnstädt--Kiss--Simon--Zilles.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a proper order scheme and restrict it to any coordinate set. Its restricted reconstruction remains in the projected class and its size does not increase. VC Dimension / Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Name: VC Dimension / Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The inequality can be strict.

Witness: SCVC/VC Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The full-domain projection is admissible by the sandwich theorem, and its VC dimension is $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$. Since $\mathrm{SCVC}$ minimizes over all admissible projections, $\mathrm{SCVC}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$.

List Replicability / VC Dimension

Name: List Replicability / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: List Replicability Number

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H). The cited result proves the stronger epsilon-wise statement $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H,\epsilon)\geq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$ for every $0<\epsilon<1/2$.

Witness: Universal Class

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The cited VC-class theorem gives $\mathrm{LR}(\mathcal H,\epsilon)\geq\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$ for every $0<\epsilon<1/2$. Since the catalogue parameter requires a single list-size bound that works for all error thresholds, it is at least each epsilon-wise lower bound, yielding the displayed inequality.

References:

[blondal2026tight]

Recursive Teaching Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Name: Recursive Teaching Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, the recursive teaching dimension is at least its Log Size Ratio.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $r=\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H)$ on an $n$-point domain. Samei--Semukhin--Yang--Zilles prove $|\mathcal H|\leq\sum_{i=0}^r\binom ni\leq(n+1)^r$. Thus $r\geq\log(|\mathcal H|)/\log(n+1)$, which is at least $\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)=\mathrm{LSR}(\mathcal H)$; singleton classes satisfy the conclusion by the catalogue's zero convention.

References:

[samei2014algebraic]

Hamming Radius / Log Size Ratio

Name: Hamming Radius / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: Hamming Radius

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, its Hamming radius is at least its Log Size Ratio.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $B=\mathrm B(\mathcal H)$ on an $n$-point domain and choose an enclosing Hamming ball of radius $B$. It contains at most $\sum_{i=0}^B\binom ni\leq(n+1)^B$ labelings. Hence $B\geq\log(|\mathcal H|)/\log(n+1)\geq\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)=\mathrm{LSR}(\mathcal H)$; the singleton case follows from the zero convention.

Littlestone Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, Littlestone dimension is at least its Log Size Ratio.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

On an $n$-point domain, use the complete depth-$n$ tree that queries the coordinates in one fixed order on every level. Its zero-cover has one distinct leaf labeling for each member of $\mathcal H$, hence size $|\mathcal H|$. Rakhlin--Sridharan--Tewari's sequential Sauer bound gives $|\mathcal H|\leq\sum_{i=0}^L\binom ni\leq(n+1)^L$ for $L=\mathrm{Ldim}(\mathcal H)$. Therefore $L\geq\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)=\mathrm{LSR}(\mathcal H)$, with the singleton convention handled separately.

References:

[rakhlin2010online]

Log Size / VC Dimension

Name: Log Size / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Log Size

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, log size is at least VC dimension.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A shattered set of size $d=\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$ realizes all $2^d$ binary traces, each supplied by a distinct member of $\mathcal H$. Thus $|\mathcal H|\geq2^d$, and taking base-two logarithms gives $\log_2|\mathcal H|\geq d$.

Log Size / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Name: Log Size / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Log Size

Parameter 2: Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, log size is at least the largest strongly shattered-set size.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A strongly shattered set of size $d$ is the free-coordinate set of a contained $d$-cube. Its $2^d$ cube vertices are distinct members of $\mathcal H$, so $|\mathcal H|\geq2^d$ and $\log_2|\mathcal H|\geq d$.

Effective Range / Log Size

Name: Effective Range / Log Size

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Log Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, effective range is at least log size.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

All concepts agree outside their effective range. Restriction to the $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ active coordinates is therefore injective on $\mathcal H$, so $|\mathcal H|\leq2^{\mathrm E(\mathcal H)}$. Taking base-two logarithms yields $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)\geq\log_2|\mathcal H|$.

Log Shattering / VC Dimension

Name: Log Shattering / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Log Shattering

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, log shattering is at least VC dimension.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If a set of size $d=\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$ is shattered, all of its $2^d$ subsets are shattered. Thus the shattering family has size at least $2^d$, and $\log_2|\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)|\geq d$.

Log Shattering / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Name: Log Shattering / Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Parameter 1: Log Shattering

Parameter 2: Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, log shattering is at least the largest strongly shattered-set size.

Witness: Singletons plus empty set

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A strongly shattered set of size $d$ contains a full $d$-cube, hence every one of its $2^d$ subsets is shattered. Therefore $|\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)|\geq2^d$ and $\log_2|\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H)|\geq d$.

Monotonic co-VC Dimension / Star Number

Name: Monotonic co-VC Dimension / Star Number

Parameter 1: Monotonic co-VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Star number

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every realized star becomes a hollow star after its centre is removed.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $h_0,h_1,\ldots,h_d$ witness a $d$-star in a projection of $\mathcal H$. In the subfamily formed by $h_1,\ldots,h_d$, the centre trace of $h_0$ is absent while each of its $d$ one-coordinate flips is present. Thus that subfamily has co-VC dimension at least $d$, and maximizing over subfamilies proves $\mathrm{coVC}^*(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm S(\mathcal H)$.

Projected Interpolation Degree / VC Dimension

Name: Projected Interpolation Degree / VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Projected Interpolation Degree

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite binary classes, projected interpolation degree equals VC dimension.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If $S$ is shattered with $|S|=d$, then $\mathcal H_{|S}=\{0,1\}^S$, whose interpolation degree is $d$; hence $\mathrm{intdeg}^p(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$. Conversely, for every projection $\mathcal G=\mathcal H_{|T}$, the established order-shattering bounds give $\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal G)\le\mathrm{OSH}_{\min}(\mathcal G)\le\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal G)\le\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$. Maximizing over $T$ gives the reverse inequality.

Effective Range / Projected Interpolation Degree

Name: Effective Range / Projected Interpolation Degree

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Projected Interpolation Degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Interpolation on an $m$-coordinate Boolean domain has degree at most $m$.

Witness: Interpolation-Conjecture Counterexample

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every projection, its effective range has at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ coordinates. Every function on a Boolean class over $m$ active coordinates is the restriction of a multilinear polynomial of degree at most $m$. Thus each projected interpolation degree is at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$, and so is their maximum.

2-Block Littlestone Dimension / $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension (affine)

Name: 2-Block Littlestone Dimension / $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: 2-Block Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: $k$-Block Littlestone Dimension ($k>2$)

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: \frac{2(k-1)}{k}

Multiplicative Constant: \frac{2}{k}

Variant:

Details:

For every fixed integer $k>2$, the complete-block comparison gives $\mathrm B_k\le\frac{k}{2}\mathrm B_2+(k-1)$, equivalently the displayed affine lower bound.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The general relation $\mathrm B_\ell\le(\ell/k)\mathrm B_k+(\ell-1)$, instantiated with the smaller block size $k=2$ and $\ell>2$, gives $\mathrm B_2\ge\frac{2}{\ell}\mathrm B_\ell-\frac{2(\ell-1)}{\ell}$. Renaming $\ell$ as the fixed parameter $k$ gives the stated bound.

Full Proof:

Full proof in the LaTeX survey: Proposition prop:block-littlestone-round-order.

Effective Range / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Effective Range / Repetition-Free Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Repetition-Free Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Repetition-Free Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, $\mathrm{rfRTD}(\mathcal H)\le E(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

On the $n$-singleton family, every coordinate is effective, so $E=n$, while the singleton concepts can be removed using their distinct one-coordinate positive teaching samples, giving $\mathrm{rfRTD}=1$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Discard the coordinates that are constant on $\mathcal H$, so the domain has $E(\mathcal H)$ coordinates. The full cube on that domain has a repetition-free teaching plan of order its domain size: recursively teach the 1-fibre using the chosen coordinate and then the 0-fibre, so the coordinate sets are all distinct. Restricting this plan to $\mathcal H$ preserves the teaching property and repetition-freeness. Hence it is a repetition-free teaching plan for $\mathcal H$ of order at most $E(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive]

Effective Range / Projected Distinguishing Range

Name: Effective Range / Projected Distinguishing Range

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Projected Distinguishing Range

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Every projection has effective range contained in that of the original class.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the two trivial concepts on n coordinates, $\mathrm E=n$ while every nonempty projection has distinguishing range one, so $\mathrm R^p=1$. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For every projection $\mathcal H_{|S}$, any distinguishing coordinate set can be chosen inside its effective range, so $\mathrm R(\mathcal H_{|S})\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H_{|S})\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$. Maximizing over S proves the claimed bound.

Size / Projected Distinguishing Range

Name: Size / Projected Distinguishing Range

Parameter 1: Size

Parameter 2: Projected Distinguishing Range

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every nonempty finite class, the stronger bound $\mathrm R^p(\mathcal H)\leq|\mathcal H|-1$ holds.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the n-dimensional full cube, $|\mathcal H|=2^n$ while $\mathrm R^p=n$. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

A projection has at most $|\mathcal H|$ distinct concepts. For any m distinct binary vectors, repeatedly add a coordinate separating two vectors still in the same trace block; each added coordinate increases the number of trace blocks, so at most m-1 coordinates distinguish all m vectors. Apply this to every projection and maximize.

Projected Distinguishing Range / Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Name: Projected Distinguishing Range / Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Projected Distinguishing Range

Parameter 2: Projected Membership Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every projection, querying a distinguishing coordinate set identifies its target.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For half-intervals, $\mathrm R^p=n-1$, while $\mathrm Q_m^p=\lceil\log_2 n\rceil$. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For each coordinate projection $\mathcal G=\mathcal H_{|S}$, query the target on a smallest coordinate set distinguishing all concepts of $\mathcal G$. The resulting trace identifies the target, hence $\mathrm Q_m(\mathcal G)\leq\mathrm R(\mathcal G)$. Taking maxima over projections gives $\mathrm Q_m^p(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm R^p(\mathcal H).

Monotonic co-VC Dimension / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Name: Monotonic co-VC Dimension / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Monotonic co-VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A minimum teaching set becomes a hollow star after its target is removed; the two monotonic envelopes preserve this comparison.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $\mathcal F$ be any coordinate projection of a subfamily of $\mathcal H$, and choose $h\in\mathcal F$ with a smallest teaching set $S$ of size $d=\mathrm{TS}_{\min}(\mathcal F)$. Minimality of $S$ gives, for every $x\in S$, a concept $h_x\in\mathcal F$ agreeing with $h$ on $S\setminus\{x\}$ and differing at $x$. In the projection of $\mathcal F\setminus\{h\}$ to $S$, the trace of $h$ is absent and all its one-coordinate flips are present. Hence $\mathrm{coVC}(\mathcal F\setminus\{h\})\ge d$. Since $\mathrm{coVC}^*$ is monotonic under both subfamilies and projections, $\mathrm{coVC}^*(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{TS}_{\min}(\mathcal F)$. Maximizing over $\mathcal F$ proves $\mathrm{coVC}^*(\mathcal H)\ge\mathrm{TS}_{\min}^{*p}(\mathcal H)$.

Star Number / Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Star Number / Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Star number

Parameter 2: Positive Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

A smallest positive teaching set at any recursive stage is a star centered at its target.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the $n$-singleton class, star number is $n-1$, while positive recursive teaching dimension is $1$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix any positive recursive teaching order. At a stage with remaining family $\mathcal G$, target $h$, and a smallest positive teaching set $S$ of size $d$, minimality gives, for each $x\in S$, a concept $g_x\in\mathcal G\setminus\{h\}$ that is one on $S\setminus\{x\}$ and zero at $x$. The target is one on all of $S$, so its trace and the traces of the $g_x$ form a $d$-star in the projection of $\mathcal G$. Since star number is subfamily-monotonic, $\mathrm S(\mathcal H)\ge d$ for every stage of every positive recursive order. It therefore bounds the minimum possible maximum stage size $\mathrm{RTD}^+(\mathcal H)$.

Radon Number / VC Dimension (logarithmic)

Name: Radon Number / VC Dimension (logarithmic)

Parameter 1: Radon Number

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge c\log P_2$

Multiplicative Constant: 1

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class with positive VC dimension, $\mathrm{Rad}(\mathcal H)\geq\lfloor\log_2(2\,\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)+2)\rfloor\geq\log_2\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For the full cube, Radon number is $\lfloor\log_2(2n+2)\rfloor$ while VC dimension is $n$, so no reverse affine-linear bound can hold.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The cited theorem gives the displayed floor-log lower bound for every class. Since $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal H)\geq1$ in the nontrivial case, it immediately implies the recorded logarithmic relation; when VC dimension is zero the logarithmic statement is vacuous.

Effective Range / Radon Number (affine)

Name: Effective Range / Radon Number (affine)

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Radon Number

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 1

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{Rad}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H)+1$.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $\mathcal T_n=\{\emptyset,[n]\}$, effective range is $n$ while the Radon number is $2$; hence no reverse affine-linear bound holds.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $R=\{h_1,\ldots,h_r\}$ be Radon independent. The singleton-versus-rest partition for $h_i$ has disjoint convex hulls, so $h_i$ is not in the version-space convex hull of $R\setminus\{h_i\}$. Hence some coordinate $x_i$ is constant on $R\setminus\{h_i\}$ and has the opposite value on $h_i$. When $r\geq3$, the coordinates $x_i$ are distinct: if $x_i=x_j$, any third member of $R$ would have to equal both opposite constant values. Every $x_i$ is active, so $r\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$. For $r\leq2$, the weaker bound $r\leq\mathrm E(\mathcal H)+1$ is immediate (two distinct concepts differ on an active coordinate).

Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Teaching Dimension

Name: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{peq}}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For half-intervals, proper equivalence-query complexity is $\log_2 n$, while teaching dimension is $2$ for $n\geq3$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a target $h$ and run an optimal proper equivalence-query learner against any legal counterexample choices. Record each returned counterexample as its labeled example $(x,h(x))$. Any $g$ agreeing with all recorded labels admits exactly the same transcript: each recorded $x$ still refutes the corresponding query for $g$. The learner therefore outputs $h$ on that transcript also when the target is $g$, so correctness forces $g=h$. The recorded examples are a teaching set for $h$, of size at most the query bound. Maximizing over $h$ gives $\mathrm{TD}\leq\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{peq}}$.

Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Star Number

Name: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity / Star Number

Parameter 1: Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 2: Star number

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{peq}}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm S(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For half-intervals, proper equivalence-query complexity is $\log_2 n$, whereas the star number (equivalently maximum projected teaching-set size) is $2$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Proper equivalence-query complexity is projection-monotone: a projected learner simulates the original learner using a lift of the projected target, and any projected counterexample is a valid counterexample to the original query. Applying $\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{peq}}\geq\mathrm{TD}$ to every coordinate projection gives $\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{peq}}(\mathcal H)\geq\max_{T\subseteq X}\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H_{|T})=\mathrm{TS}_{\max}^p(\mathcal H)$. The latter parameter equals the star number.

Full Proof:

Database-owned proper-equivalence-query/teaching argument, projection-monotonicity evidence for Proper Equivalence Queries Complexity, and the Star Number / Maximum Projected Teaching Set Size equality.

Projected Distinguishing Range / Star Number

Name: Projected Distinguishing Range / Star Number

Parameter 1: Projected Distinguishing Range

Parameter 2: Star number

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm R^p(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm S(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For half-intervals, projected distinguishing range is $n-1$, while the star number is $2$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $x_1,\ldots,x_s$ and $h_0,h_1,\ldots,h_s$ witness an $s$-star. Project to $T=\{x_1,\ldots,x_s\}$. In $\mathcal H_{|T}$, $h_i$ differs from the center $h_0$ exactly at $x_i$. Any coordinate set whose traces distinguish every projected concept must therefore contain every $x_i$, or it fails to distinguish $h_0$ from $h_i$. Thus $\mathrm R(\mathcal H_{|T})\geq s$; maximizing over $T$ proves $\mathrm R^p\geq\mathrm S$.

Maximum Projected Teaching Set Size / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Maximum Projected Teaching Set Size / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Maximum Projected Teaching Set Size

Parameter 2: Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{TS}_{\max}^p(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{RTD}^p(\mathcal H)$. Equivalently, the star number upper-bounds projected recursive teaching dimension.

Witness: Singletons

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $n$ singleton concepts, maximum projected teaching-set size (equivalently star number) is $n-1$, while projected recursive teaching dimension is $1$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For each coordinate projection $\mathcal H_{|S}$, recursive teaching dimension is at most teaching dimension, because a teaching set for every target in the full projected class is available at the first recursive stage. Taking the maximum over $S$ gives $\max_S\mathrm{RTD}(\mathcal H_{|S})\leq\max_S\mathrm{TD}(\mathcal H_{|S})=\mathrm{TS}_{\max}^p(\mathcal H)$.

Diameter / Sign Rank (affine)

Name: Diameter / Sign Rank (affine)

Parameter 1: Diameter

Parameter 2: Sign Rank

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1/2

Multiplicative Constant: 1/2

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary concept class, $\mathrm{SR}(\mathcal H)\leq2\mathrm D(\mathcal H)+1$.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $\mathcal T_n=\{\emptyset,[n]\}$, diameter is $n$ while sign rank is $1$, so no reverse affine-linear bound holds.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix $h_0\in\mathcal H$ and flip every sign-matrix column on which $h_0$ is positive. The row for $h$ is then positive exactly on $S_h=h\triangle h_0$, whose size is at most $D=\mathrm D(\mathcal H)$. Assign distinct real numbers $t_x$ to the coordinates. If $S_h$ is neither empty nor the whole domain, choose $0<\varepsilon_h<\min_{x\notin S_h}\prod_{s\in S_h}(t_x-t_s)^2$ and use the polynomial $p_h(t)=\varepsilon_h-\prod_{s\in S_h}(t-t_s)^2$. It is positive exactly at the $t_s$ for $s\in S_h$ and has degree at most $2D$. Constant negative and positive polynomials handle $S_h=\emptyset$ and the full domain. The evaluation matrix $(p_h(t_x))$ factors through the $2D+1$ monomials $1,t,\ldots,t^{2D}$, so it has rank at most $2D+1$ and realizes the flipped sign pattern. Column flips preserve sign rank.

Hamming Radius / Repetition-Free Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Hamming Radius / Repetition-Free Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Hamming Radius

Parameter 2: Repetition-Free Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, $\mathrm B(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{rfRTD}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $\mathcal T_n=\{\emptyset,[n]\}$, the Hamming radius is $\lceil n/2\rceil$, while the two concepts have a repetition-free recursive teaching plan of order one.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Let $T$ be a centre of an enclosing Hamming ball of radius $B$, and assign to each $h\in\mathcal H$ the coordinate set $r(h)=h\mathbin\Delta T$. Then $|r(h)|\le B$. This map is non-clashing: if $h$ and $g$ differ at $x$, exactly one of them differs from $T$ at $x$, so $x\in r(h)\cup r(g)$. Order the distinct sets $r(h)$ by reverse inclusion, breaking incomparable pairs arbitrarily. For an earlier $h$ and later $g$, $r(h)\not\subseteq r(g)$, so some $x\in r(h)\setminus r(g)$ distinguishes them. Thus the representation map is acyclic non-clashing. The representation-map/teaching-plan equivalence yields a repetition-free teaching plan of order at most $B$.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive]

Projected Distinguishing Range / Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Name: Projected Distinguishing Range / Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Parameter 1: Projected Distinguishing Range

Parameter 2: Proper Ordered Sample Compression

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class, $\mathrm R^p(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{pOSC}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Half-intervals

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For half-intervals on $[n]$, $\mathrm R^p=n-1$, whereas the proper ordered sample-compression value is one.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Fix a proper ordered scheme and consider any inclusion-minimal teaching sample used by its compression rule, for a hypothesis $h$ relative to the later hypotheses in the defining order. Write $T$ for its coordinate set. For each $x\in T$, minimality supplies a later hypothesis $h_x$ agreeing with $h$ on $T\setminus\{x\}$ and differing at $x$. Thus the projection of the relevant tail class to $T$ contains the star consisting of $h_{|T}$ and the $|T|$ distinct one-coordinate flips $(h_x)_{|T}$. Every coordinate of this star is needed to distinguish its centre from its corresponding leaf, so its distinguishing range is $|T|$. The full projected class has distinguishing range at least that of this subfamily, hence $\mathrm R^p(\mathcal H)\geq|T|$. This holds for every compressed minimal teaching sample, so it bounds the scheme size; minimizing over proper ordered schemes gives $\mathrm R^p\geq\mathrm{pOSC}$.

References:

[darnstadt2016order]

Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Name: Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension / Log Size Ratio

Parameter 1: Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For every finite non-singleton binary class.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If an admissible projection on $m$ coordinates has VC dimension $d$, its shattered-set family is downward closed with all faces of size at most $d$. Hence $|\mathcal H|\leq|\operatorname{sh}(\mathcal H_{|S})|\leq\sum_{i=0}^d\binom mi\leq(m+1)^d\leq(n+1)^d$. Taking logarithms yields $\mathrm{SCVC}(\mathcal H)\geq\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)$.

Full Proof:

Proposition \ref{prop:scvc-log-size-ratio} in the survey.

Worst Mistakes / Best Mistakes

Name: Worst Mistakes / Best Mistakes

Parameter 1: Worst Mistakes

Parameter 2: Best Mistakes

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

An adversarial presentation is at least as hard as a presentation chosen favorably to the learner.

Witness: Best/Worst Mistakes Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For C_bw, the best fixed-order transductive mistake bound is 1 while the worst fixed-order bound is 2; sync/verify_best_worst_mistakes_gap.py exhausts the six coordinate orders.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The worst-mistakes game takes the maximum over all presentation sequences, whereas the best-mistakes game permits a favorable choice of sequence. Taking a maximum over a superset of sequences cannot decrease the optimal worst-case mistake count, so $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{worst}}(\mathcal{H})\ge\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{best}}(\mathcal{H})$.

References:

[ben1997online]

Occasional One-Sided Getback / Best Mistakes

Name: Occasional One-Sided Getback / Best Mistakes

Parameter 1: Occasional One-Sided Getback

Parameter 2: Best Mistakes

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

O1G has optional mistake-only feedback, while the favorable mistake model supplies full labels.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Any favorable-sequence full-feedback strategy can be used in the best-mistakes model, whereas an O1G learner must operate when feedback may be withheld and every supplied signal only identifies a mistake. The full-feedback model can simulate O1G and may reveal strictly more information, so $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{best}}(\mathcal H)\le\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{o1g}}(\mathcal H)$.

References:

[ben1997online]

Worst Mistakes / Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Name: Worst Mistakes / Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: Worst Mistakes

Parameter 2: Partial Equivalence Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes, $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{worst}}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm Q_{\mathrm{par}}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness: Addressing

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For $\mathrm{ADDRESSING}_n$, worst mistakes is $\lfloor\log_2 n\rfloor$, while partial equivalence queries identify the target with one query by proposing the partial hypothesis that is negative on every address point and undefined on bit points. Hence the gap is unbounded.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Corollary 8(1) of Doliwa--Fan--Simon--Zilles states $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{partial}}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{Mopt}(\mathcal H)$. Their $\mathrm{Mopt}$ is the optimal adversarial online mistake bound, which is precisely the catalogue's $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{worst}}$, while $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{partial}}$ is its partial-equivalence-query complexity.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive]

Worst Mistakes / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Name: Worst Mistakes / Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Worst Mistakes

Parameter 2: Projected Recursive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

For finite concept classes, $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{worst}}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{RTD}^{p}(\mathcal H)$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Corollary 8(2) of Doliwa--Fan--Simon--Zilles states $\mathrm{RTD}^{*}(\mathcal H)\leq\mathrm{Mopt}(\mathcal H)$. Their $\mathrm{RTD}^{*}$ is the maximum recursive teaching dimension over coordinate projections, the catalogue's projected recursive teaching dimension, and $\mathrm{Mopt}$ is the catalogue's worst-mistakes parameter.

Projected No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / VC Dimension (affine)

No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Log Size

Name: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Log Size

Parameter 1: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size

Relationship Type: $P_1 \ge \frac{cP_2}{\log n}$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

For every finite binary class $\mathcal H$ with $\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)\geq2$, $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\geq\log_2|\mathcal H|/(2\log_2\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H))$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Put $d=\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)$. Padding a width-$d$ no-clashing teacher gives each concept a labeled sample on exactly $d$ of the $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ effective coordinates. Distinct concepts receive distinct samples, so $|\mathcal H|\leq2^d\binom{\mathrm E(\mathcal H)}d\leq(2\mathrm E(\mathcal H))^d$. Taking binary logarithms and using $1+\log_2\mathrm E\leq2\log_2\mathrm E$ gives the claim.

Full Proof:

Proposition prop:nctd-low-log-size in the LaTeX survey.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Effective Range / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension (affine)

Name: Effective Range / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 2

Variant:

Details:

Every finite binary class satisfies $\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)\geq2\,\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)-1$, equivalently $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\leq\lceil\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)/2\rceil$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For the full cube on $n$ active coordinates, $\mathrm{E}=n$ and $\mathrm{NCTD}=\lceil n/2\rceil$, so the affine bound is attained for odd $n$ and is off by only one for even $n$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

After removing inactive coordinates, view $\mathcal H$ as a subfamily of the full cube on $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$ coordinates. No-clashing teaching dimension is monotonic under subfamilies, while Theorem 19 of Kirkpatrick--Simon--Zilles gives $\mathrm{NCTD}(2^{[m]})=\lceil m/2\rceil$. Thus $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\leq\lceil\mathrm E(\mathcal H)/2\rceil$, which is equivalent to the stated affine inequality.

Full Proof:

Proposition prop:effective-range-low-nctd in the LaTeX survey.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Effective Range / Projected No-Clashing Teaching Dimension (affine)

Name: Effective Range / Projected No-Clashing Teaching Dimension (affine)

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Projected No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Additive Constant: 1

Multiplicative Constant: 2

Variant:

Details:

Every finite binary class satisfies $\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)\geq2\,\mathrm{NCTD}^{p}(\mathcal H)-1$, equivalently $\mathrm{NCTD}^{p}(\mathcal H)\leq\lceil\mathrm{E}(\mathcal H)/2\rceil$.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Witness Verification:

For the full cube on $n$ active coordinates, $\mathrm{E}=n$ and $\mathrm{NCTD}^{p}=\lceil n/2\rceil$, so the affine bound is attained for odd $n$ and is off by only one for even $n$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Each coordinate trace $\mathcal H_{\mid S}$ has effective range at most $\mathrm E(\mathcal H)$. Applying the sharp no-clashing upper bound $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H_{\mid S})\leq\lceil\mathrm E(\mathcal H)/2\rceil$ to every trace and maximizing over $S$ proves the claim.

Full Proof:

Corollary cor:effective-range-low-projected-nctd in the LaTeX survey.
