No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Log Size Ratio (affine)

Name: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Log Size Ratio (affine)

Parameter 1: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

Every finite nontrivial binary class satisfies $\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)\geq\tfrac12\,\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)$, where $n$ is the ambient domain size.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

If the effective range has size at least two, the no-clashing counting bound gives $\mathrm{NCTD}\geq\log|\mathcal H|/(2\log\mathrm E)$. Since $\mathrm E\leq n$ and $\log(|\mathcal H|-1)\leq\log|\mathcal H|$, this dominates one half of the log size ratio. If $\mathrm E=1$, a nontrivial binary class has two concepts and the log size ratio is zero, so the claim is immediate.

Full Proof:

Corollary cor:nctd-low-log-size-ratio in the LaTeX survey.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Log Size Ratio (affine)

Name: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension / Log Size Ratio (affine)

Parameter 1: Positive No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge cP_2-d$

Multiplicative Constant: \tfrac12

Variant:

Details:

Every finite nontrivial binary class satisfies $\mathrm{NCTD}^{+}(\mathcal H)\geq\tfrac12\,\log(|\mathcal H|-1)/\log(n+1)$, where $n$ is the ambient domain size.

Witness Strength:

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Every positive no-clashing teacher is in particular a no-clashing teacher. Thus $\mathrm{NCTD}^{+}(\mathcal H)\geq\mathrm{NCTD}(\mathcal H)$, and the no-clashing counting argument gives the stated log-size-ratio lower bound directly.

Full Proof:

Corollary cor:positive-nctd-low-log-size-ratio in the LaTeX survey.

References:

[KSZ2019]

Established incomparabilities

Pairs for which neither parameter dominates the other; each record supplies separation witnesses in both directions.

co-VC Dimension / Hitting Size (Incomparable)

Name: co-VC Dimension / Hitting Size (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: co-VC dimension

Parameter 2: Hitting Size

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite binary classes.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For $\mathcal C^{\cap}_n=\{\{0,i\}:1\leq i\leq n\}$, $\mathrm{coVC}=n$ after projecting away the common coordinate, whereas $\mathrm{HT}=1$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For the full cube $2^{[n]}$, $\mathrm{HT}=n$ because every singleton must be hit, whereas $\mathrm{coVC}=0$ because every trace is realized.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The two explicit families give unbounded gaps in the two opposite directions.

References:

Database-owned value proofs for Common-Core Singletons and the Full Cube.

Subset Teaching Dimension / VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Name: Subset Teaching Dimension / VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Subset Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter admits a universal upper bound in terms of the other, even up to an affine-linear factor.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For every $u\ge3$, $\mathcal C^u_{1/2}=\{\{x_i\}:i\in[u+1]\}\cup\{\{x_i,x_j\}:i,j\in[u+1]\}$ has $\mathrm{STD}=u$ and $\mathrm{VC}=2$: pairs are shattered, while a triple cannot be because every concept has size at most two.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

The class of monomials over $m$ Boolean variables has $\mathrm{STD}=2$ and $\mathrm{VC}\ge m$. The $m$ assignments with exactly one zero coordinate are shattered by positive conjunctions.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Theorem 18(2) gives $\mathrm{STD}(\mathcal C^u_{1/2})=u$, and the displayed size-two construction gives its VC dimension. Theorem 19 gives constant STD for monomials; the stated explicit set is shattered, so their VC dimension is unbounded.

References:

[zilles2011models]

Subset Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension Incomparability

Name: Subset Teaching Dimension / No-Clashing Teaching Dimension Incomparability

Parameter 1: Subset Teaching Dimension

Parameter 2: No-Clashing Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

For finite binary concept classes, neither parameter admits a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For every $u\ge2$, the family $\mathcal C^u_{1/2}$ of all singleton and doubleton subsets of a $(u+1)$-point domain has $\mathrm{STD}=u$ and $\mathrm{NCTD}\le\mathrm{RTD}=2$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

Proposition 17(i) gives, for every $n$, a generalized paired family with $\mathrm{STD}=1$ and $\mathrm{NCTD}=n$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Theorem 36 of Zilles--Lange--Holte--Zinkevich gives the first family with $\mathrm{STD}=u$ and $\mathrm{RTD}=2$; no-clashing teaching dimension is at most recursive teaching dimension. Mansouri--Simon--Singla--Zilles, Proposition 17(i), constructs the reverse unbounded separation.

Full Proof:

Zilles et al., Theorem 36; Mansouri et al., Proposition 17(i)

References:

[zilles2011models] [KSZ2019] [mansouri2022batch]

Diameter / Distinguishing Range (Incomparable)

Name: Diameter / Distinguishing Range (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Diameter

Parameter 2: Distinguishing Range

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite classes.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For the two-concept class $\mathcal T_n=\{\emptyset,[n]\}$, $\mathrm D(\mathcal T_n)=n$ while $\mathrm R(\mathcal T_n)=1$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For the singleton class $\mathcal S_n=\{\{i\}:i\in[n]\}$, $\mathrm R(\mathcal S_n)=n-1$ while $\mathrm D(\mathcal S_n)=2$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For $\mathcal T_n$, its sole distinct pair differs on all $n$ coordinates, whereas one coordinate already distinguishes its two concepts. For $\mathcal S_n$, any two concepts differ on exactly two coordinates; retaining any $n-1$ coordinates gives every singleton a different trace, and omitting two coordinates makes the corresponding singleton traces equal. These are the canonical value calculations recorded for Trivial Concepts and Singletons.

Full Proof:

Database-owned value proofs: Diameter and Distinguishing Range of Trivial Concepts; Diameter and Distinguishing Range of Singletons (generated in the LaTeX value-proofs catalogue).

Diameter / co-VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Name: Diameter / co-VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Diameter

Parameter 2: co-VC dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite classes.

For the half-interval class $\mathcal I_n$, projected distinguishing range is $n-1$, while log shattering is $\log_2 n$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For the trivial class $\mathcal T_n=\{\emptyset,[n]\}$, log shattering is $\log_2(n+1)$, while projected distinguishing range is $1$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The two canonical value calculations give unbounded gaps in the two directions.

Full Proof:

Database-owned value proofs for Projected Distinguishing Range and Log Shattering of Half-Intervals and Trivial Concepts.

Parameter 1: Sign Rank

Parameter 2: Radon Number

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

Status: Established

Largest Strongly Shattered Set / co-VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Name: Largest Strongly Shattered Set / co-VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Largest Strongly Shattered Set

Parameter 2: co-VC dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite binary classes.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For the full cube $2^{[n]}$, the whole domain is a contained $n$-cube, so $\mathrm{ST}=n$, while $\mathrm{coVC}=0$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For common-core singletons $\mathcal C^{\cap}_n=\{\{0,i\}:1\leq i\leq n\}$, $\mathrm{coVC}=n$ after projecting away the common coordinate, while $\mathrm{ST}=0$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The two explicit families give unbounded gaps in the two opposite directions.

References:

Database-owned value proofs for the Full Cube and Common-Core Singletons.

Minimum Degree / co-VC Dimension (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Minimum Degree

Parameter 2: co-VC dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite binary classes.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For the full cube $2^{[n]}$, every vertex has degree $n$, whereas $\mathrm{coVC}=0$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For common-core singletons $\mathcal C^{\cap}_n=\{\{0,i\}:1\leq i\leq n\}$, $\mathrm{coVC}=n$ after projecting away the common coordinate, whereas the one-inclusion graph is edgeless and $\mathrm d_{\min}=0$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The two explicit families give unbounded gaps in the two opposite directions.

References:

Database-owned value proofs for the Full Cube and Common-Core Singletons.

Dual VC Dimension / Minimum Degree (Incomparable)

Name: Dual VC Dimension / Minimum Degree (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Dual VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Minimum Degree

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite binary classes.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For the universal class $\mathcal U_n$, $\mathrm{VC}^*=n$, while its one-inclusion graph is edgeless and $\mathrm d_{\min}=0$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For the full cube $2^{[n]}$, $\mathrm d_{\min}=n$, whereas $\mathrm{VC}^*=\lfloor\log_2n\rfloor$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The universal-class and full-cube calculations give unbounded gaps in the two opposite directions.

Dual VC Dimension / Log Size Ratio (Incomparable)

Name: Dual VC Dimension / Log Size Ratio (Incomparable)

Parameter 1: Dual VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Log Size Ratio

Relationship Type: $P_1 \parallel P_2$ (incomparable)

Variant:

Details:

Neither parameter has a universal affine-linear upper bound in terms of the other, even for finite binary classes.

Witness Strength:

Witness: Parameter 1 Larger:

For the universal class $\mathcal U_n$, $\mathrm{VC}^*=n$, while the log size ratio is $\log(n-1)/\log(2^n+1)=o(1)$.

Witness: Parameter 2 Larger:

For the full cube $2^{[n]}$, the log size ratio is $\log(2^n-1)/\log(n+1)=\Theta(n/\log n)$, while $\mathrm{VC}^*=\lfloor\log_2n\rfloor$.

Status: Established

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The universal-class and full-cube value calculations give gaps whose ratios diverge in the two opposite directions.

References:

Database-owned value proofs; the dual-VC values also follow from [chornomaz2025spherical].

Details:

Refuted relationships

Claims recorded with a counterexample; they are not asserted as facts.

Proper Stable Labeled Sample Compression / co-VC dimension

Name: Proper Stable Labeled Sample Compression / co-VC dimension

Parameter 1: Proper Stable Labeled Sample Compression

Parameter 2: co-VC dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Contradicted by the trivial two-concept class: proper stable labeled compression is 1, whereas co-VC dimension is 2.

Witness: Trivial concepts

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For the two constant concepts on a two-point domain, retaining one labeled example gives a proper stable labeled compression scheme, so the compression value is 1. The two traces form a hollow 2-star (the two mixed labelings are absent), so co-VC dimension is 2. Hence the stated universal inequality is false.

Order Sample Compression / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Name: Order Sample Compression / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Order Sample Compression

Parameter 2: Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Refuted. On the three-code class, $\mathrm{OSC}=1$ but $\mathrm{TS}_{\min}^{*p}=2$. The cited order-compression theorem controls projected recursive teaching dimension for the given class; it does not justify maximizing a separate minimum-teaching quantity over all subclasses.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The three-code class $\{000,011,101\}$ has an order scheme of size one, using the order $000<011<101$. Yet after omitting, respectively, $x_0,x_1,x_2$, each of its three targets is the middle point of a three-trace path and needs two coordinates to be taught. Thus $\mathrm{TS}_{\min}^{*p}=2>1=\mathrm{OSC}$.

References:

[darnstadt2016order]

Littlestone Dimension / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Name: Littlestone Dimension / Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Parameter 1: Littlestone Dimension

Parameter 2: Monotonic Projected Minimum Teaching Set Size

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Refuted. The three-code class has $\mathrm{Ldim}=1$ and $\mathrm{TS}_{\min}^{*p}=2$. The cited twofold-monotonicity statement does not yield a bound by the maximum-over-subclasses projected teaching quantity used here.

Witness: Three-Code Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The three-code class $\{000,011,101\}$ has no complete Littlestone tree of depth two, hence $\mathrm{Ldim}=1$. On the other hand, omitting a different coordinate makes each target the middle of a three-trace path, so its minimum projected teaching-set size is two and therefore $\mathrm{TS}_{\min}^{*p}=2$. Hence $\mathrm{Ldim}<\mathrm{TS}_{\min}^{*p}$.

References:

[doliwa2014recursive]

Invalid as stated. The family $\mathcal H=\{\{1\},\{2\}\}$ has oriented diameter $0$ and diameter $2$.

Witness Strength:

Proof or Proof Sketch:

On its two-coordinate domain, this family has no projection containing both $\varnothing$ and its full coordinate set, so $\mathrm D^+(\mathcal H)=0$. Its two concepts differ on both coordinates, hence $\mathrm D(\mathcal H)=2$. No positive universal constant can make the displayed inequality hold.

Log Size / Sign Rank

Name: Log Size / Sign Rank

Parameter 1: Log Size

Parameter 2: Sign Rank

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Invalid as stated. If the rows are the concepts of an $N\times N$ Hadamard sign matrix, then $|\mathcal H|=N$ but its sign rank is at least $\sqrt N$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Interpret the rows of an $N\times N$ Hadamard matrix as concepts on its $N$ columns. Forster's spectral lower bound gives sign rank at least $\sqrt N$. For arbitrarily large powers of two, $\sqrt N>\log_2N$, contradicting the displayed inequality.

References:

[forster2002linear]

Effective Range / Positive Teaching Dimension

Name: Effective Range / Positive Teaching Dimension

Parameter 1: Effective Range

Parameter 2: Positive Teaching Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Refuted: positive teaching dimension need not be finite, even on a finite domain.

Witness: Full Cube

Witness Strength: Unbounded gap

Witness Verification:

For every $n\ge1$, the full cube has effective range $n$ and positive teaching dimension $\infty$ because the empty concept has no finite positive teaching set. Thus the proposed reverse inequality fails more strongly than by an unbounded finite gap.

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

For the full cube $2^{[n]}$, the empty concept has no positive examples. Its only positive sample is therefore empty, which every cube concept realizes; it cannot be a positive teaching set. Hence $\mathrm{TD}^+(2^{[n]})=\infty$, while the effective range is $n$.

References:

[balbach2008measuring]

VC Dimension / Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Name: VC Dimension / Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Parameter 1: VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Self-Directed Queries Complexity

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

Refuted: self-directed query complexity has no upper bound that depends only on VC dimension. Ben-David--Eiron construct, for every fixed $d\geq3$ and arbitrarily large $n$, a finite class $\mathcal C^d_n$ with $\mathrm{VC}(\mathcal C^d_n)=d$ and $\mathrm{SDC}(\mathcal C^d_n)=\Theta(n)$.

Witness Strength:

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

Lemma 1 of the cited paper constructs the classes $\mathcal C^d_n$ and establishes the displayed fixed-VC, unbounded-self-directed-complexity behavior. Consequently no function of VC dimension alone can universally upper-bound $\mathrm{SDC}$.

References:

[ben1998self]

Interpolation Degree / Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Name: Interpolation Degree / Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Parameter 1: Interpolation degree

Parameter 2: Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Relationship Type: $P_1 = P_2$

Variant:

Details:

The draft conjecture $\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal H)=\mathrm{SCVC}(\mathcal H)$ is false. On $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{int}}=\{001,010,011,100\}$, the two quantities are respectively $1$ and $2$. The reverse one-sided comparison is separately refuted by $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{rev}}$.

Witness: Interpolation-Conjecture Counterexample

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The constant polynomial and the three coordinate functions have linearly independent evaluation vectors on the four concepts, so $\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{int}})=1$. The projection to $\{x_2,x_3\}$ is the full two-cube, so it is admissible and has VC dimension two. No projection to at most one coordinate has four shattered subsets, while the full projection has five; hence every admissible projection has VC dimension at least two and $\mathrm{SCVC}(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{int}})=2$.

Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension / Interpolation Degree

Name: Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension / Interpolation Degree

Parameter 1: Shattering-Cover Projection VC Dimension

Parameter 2: Interpolation degree

Relationship Type: $P_1\ge P_2$

Variant:

Details:

No reverse one-sided comparison rescues the refuted equality: on $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{rev}}=\{0110,0111,1000,1001\}$, $\mathrm{SCVC}=1$ while $\mathrm{intdeg}=2$.

Witness: SCVC/Interpolation Reverse Separation Class

Witness Strength: Strict separation

Status: Refuted

Proof or Proof Sketch:

The projection to $\{x_1,x_2,x_3\}$ has trace $\{011,100\}$, whose empty set and three singleton coordinates are shattered, while its VC dimension is one; it is therefore SCVC-admissible. Degree-one polynomials have only rank three on the four original concepts, whereas degree-two polynomials have rank four. Thus $\mathrm{SCVC}(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{rev}})=1<2=\mathrm{intdeg}(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{rev}})$.
